Administrative Structure
As already described for Study TAK-861-3001, Study TAK-861-3002 also had:
•

DMC: An oveporexton program-level external DMC reviewed unblinded safety data
quarterly and had access to trial data for safety assessment

•

LSEC: Independent from the DMC, reviewed cases of possible DILI, trial intervention
discontinuations due to liver-related adverse events, and liver-related serious adverse
events

•

Trial Monitoring: Sites were monitored by
with visits conducted per the
site monitoring plan to review protocol adherence, GCP compliance, and data
completeness, accuracy, and consistency
(b) (4)

171
Reference ID: 5847402

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 220860
Orzeyful (Oveporexton)
•

Investigator Training: A Sponsor representative trained investigators on GCP compliance
and trial protocol conduct

•

Trial Intervention Accountability: The investigator or designee maintained 100%
accountability for all trial interventions during participation

•

Quality Management: Each clinical trial site was responsible for internal quality
management of trial conduct, data, and biological specimen collection, documentation, and
completion

•

Central Laboratories: Accredited central laboratories ensured consistency of analyses and
reporting for clinical laboratory tests

•

Audits: Audits of investigator trial sites and other facilities were included as part of
independent Takeda quality assessment

•

Randomization: A designated vendor generated the randomization schedule using the
permuted block method with stratification factors (region and prior use of medication for
NT1).

The trial design for TAK-861-3002 was generally adequate and consistent with the overall
development program. The selection of the 2-mg BID dosage was based on the efficacy and
safety/tolerability profile observed in the phase 2 trial (TAK-861-2001), which is appropriate.
Of note, Study 3002 is identical to Study 3001 with the exception of the dosages tested (only
the higher dosage of 2 mg BID is tested in 3002), the number of arms (just two), and the
regions where the studies were conducted (none in the United States).
The randomization ratio of 1:2 (placebo: oveporexton 2 mg BID) was acceptable and aligned
with the goal of maximizing enrollment to the active treatment group while maintaining a
placebo control. This allocation strategy is reasonable for a rare disease like NT1, as it allows
for greater safety data collection on the active treatment while minimizing the number of
participants receiving placebo.
Blinding procedures were adequate, with all participants receiving the same number of
tablets and bottles regardless of treatment assignment.
The exclusion of medications that could affect EDS or cataplexy was appropriate to isolate the
treatment effect of oveporexton. The exclusion of medications associated with severe HI
remained appropriate given the ongoing hepatotoxicity concerns related to the structurally
similar compound firazorexton.
As with Study TAK-861-3001, the program-level DMC and LSEC provided appropriate safety
oversight throughout the trial, which was particularly important given the hepatotoxicity
signal from firazorexton.
Study Endpoints
The endpoint structure for Study TAK-861-3002 was nearly identical to that of Study TAK-8613001, as previously described.

172
Reference ID: 5847402

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 220860
Orzeyful (Oveporexton)
Primary Endpoint
As in Study TAK-861-3001, the primary endpoint was:
•

Change from Baseline to Week 12 in mean sleep latency from four MWT wakefulness trials

The key difference from Study TAK-861-3001 is that TAK-861-3002 evaluated only the 2-mg
BID dosage compared to placebo, whereas Study TAK-861-3001 evaluated both the 1-mg BID
and 2-mg BID dosages.
Secondary Endpoints
The secondary endpoints were the same as in Study TAK-861-3001, grouped into Family 1 and
Family 2 for multiplicity control:
•

Family 1: Change from Baseline to Week 12 in ESS total score and weekly cataplexy rate
(WCR) at Week 12

•

Family 2: Change from Baseline to Week 12 in mean number of lapses on PVT, PGI-C score
at Week 12, change from Baseline to Week 12 in NSS-CT total score, change from Baseline
to Week 12 in all six FINI domain scores (Tiredness, Cognitive Functioning, Cataplexy, Social
Activities, Everyday Activities, and Everyday Responsibilities), and change from Baseline to
Week 12 in SF-36 mental and physical component summary scores.

Exploratory Endpoints
The exploratory endpoints were consistent with those in Study TAK-861-3001, with one
notable addition: Study TAK-861-3002 included the Work Productivity and Activity
Impairment:Narcolepsy questionnaire, which was not included in Study TAK-861-3001.
The WPAI:Narcolepsy is a participant-reported 6-item questionnaire that measures work time
missed and work and activity impairment due to narcolepsy during the past 7 days. The
instrument yields four scores: absenteeism, presenteeism, work productivity loss, and activity
impairment due to NT1. Work impairment was evaluated among employed participants and
activity impairment outside of work was assessed among all participants. For each score, the
corresponding WPAI:Narcolepsy outcome is expressed as a percentage, with higher numbers
indicating greater impairment and less productivity. This additional endpoint provided
information on the impact of oveporexton on work productivity and activity impairment in
employed participants.
All other exploratory endpoints (CGI-C/CGI-S/PGI-S, NSS-CT individual questions, nPSG,
eDiary, cognitive tests, KSS, BC-CCI-E, TSQM, EQ-5D-5L, and SF-36 domain scores) were the
same as in Study TAK-861-3001.
Statistical Analysis Plan
The estimand definitions, derivations, and analysis methods were generally the same as for
Study TAK-861-3001, described previously. The only exception is the multiple testing
173
Reference ID: 5847402

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 220860
Orzeyful (Oveporexton)
approach, which was simpler due to the fact that the Study TAK-861-3001 only contained one
active arm. Except for the difference in number of active arms, the general multiple testing
approach and order of endpoints remained the same, with the same two families. The
hierarchical multiple testing procedure employed a graphical testing approach.
The primary and secondary efficacy endpoints were grouped into two families, where family 1
included hypotheses associated with change from Baseline to Week 12 in mean sleep latency
on the MWT, change from Baseline to Week 12 in ESS total score, and WCR at Week 12.
Family 2 included hypotheses associated with the remaining secondary efficacy endpoints
(change from Baseline to Week 12 in mean number of lapses on the PVT, PGI-C score at Week
12, change from Baseline to Week 12 in NSS-CT total score, change from Baseline to Week 12
in six FINI domain scores, and change from Baseline to Week 12 in SF-36 mental and physical
component summary scores). The multiple testing approach for each family is presented in
Figure 18 and Figure 19, respectively.

Protocol Amendments
The initial protocol was amended once locally for the EU (September 6, 2024) and once
globally (October 29, 2024). The first participant was enrolled on November 7, 2024, under
the original protocol.
Noteworthy changes included:
Initial Protocol EU Version 1 (September 6, 2024):
•

Changed the primary endpoint for EDS assessment from MWT to ESS total score exclusively
in the EU/EEA (MWT became a secondary endpoint in EU/EEA only).

•

Clarified the exploratory objective as "To assess the effect of oveporexton on quantity and
quality of sleep" (instead of only nocturnal sleep) and updated the corresponding endpoint
to include daytime and nocturnal sleep quantity and quality questions from the eDiary.

•

Clarified that pregnant participants were to be withdrawn from treatment but preferably
not from the trial.
175

Reference ID: 5847402

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 220860
Orzeyful (Oveporexton)
•

Clarified analysis set definitions: safety set analyzed according to treatment received; full
analysis set (FAS) analyzed according to randomized treatment assignment.

•

Moved the PVT number of lapses endpoint from Family 1 to Family 2.

•

Added a section on missing data handling

Global Protocol Amendment 1 (October 29, 2024):
•

Incorporated the EU-specific changes regarding ESS as primary endpoint in EU/EEA.

•

Specified that primary and secondary endpoints (MWT, ESS, NSS-CT, FINI, SF-36) were
measured as change from Baseline at Week 12, as per Agency’s recommendation.

•

Changed PVT assessment from single 3 PM session to mean of 2 intraday sessions.

•

Specified components of the eye examination within the physical examination to be
performed at Baseline and at subsequent visits.

•

Allowed ESS retesting after medication discontinuation/tapering if screening ESS was <11 in
participants taking NT1 medications.

•

Clarified that pregnant participants were to be withdrawn from treatment but preferably
not from the trial.

•

Clarified that all participants in the safety set were to be analyzed according to the
treatment they received.

•

Clarified that the FAS consisted of all participants who were randomized and all participants
in the FAS were to be analyzed according to the treatment group to which they were
randomized.

•

The endpoint of number of lapses on the PVT was moved from Family 1 to Family 2.

•

Added a section on missing data handling. Clarified that placebo-based multiple imputation
would be used to handle missing data due to ICEs in the primary analysis.

The protocol amendments do not appear to have substantially affected the conduct or
integrity of the trial. The first participant was enrolled on November 7, 2024, under the
original protocol, and the amendments were implemented relatively early in the trial
timeline.
The Applicant implemented Global Protocol Amendment 1 primarily in response to FDA
feedback. Notably, during the development program, the Agency informed the Applicant that
the ESS was not considered an optimal primary endpoint because it is a subjective measure,
and recommended the MWT as the preferred primary endpoint due to its objective nature.
The EU-specific amendment, which designated ESS as the primary endpoint exclusively in the
EU/EEA, reflects regional regulatory differences but does not impact the U.S. regulatory
assessment, where MWT remains the primary endpoint.
Additionally, at the EOP2 meeting, the Agency recommended that the Applicant change their
MWT endpoint from assessment at Week 12 to change from Baseline at Week 12, as the
trajectory of change over time provides important information for labeling to inform
176
Reference ID: 5847402

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 220860
Orzeyful (Oveporexton)
healthcare providers about the expected treatment response pattern. This recommendation
was incorporated into Global Protocol Amendment 1, which specified that primary and
secondary endpoints were to be measured as change from Baseline at Week 12.
The other amendments primarily consisted of clarifications to analysis set definitions, missing
data handling procedures, specification of eye examination components, and minor
adjustments to assessment timing (e.g., PVT measurement from two intraday sessions rather
than a single session), none of which appear to compromise the trial's scientific validity or
interpretability.
8.1.2.2.

Study Results

Compliance With Good Clinical Practices
The Applicant states that “This trial was conducted with the highest respect for the individual
participants in accordance with the protocol and ethical principles that have their origins in
the Declaration of Helsinki and in compliance with IRB regulations stated in Title 21 of the US
Code of Federal Regulations Part 56, ICH Harmonised Tripartite Guideline for GCP E6, IECs and
EU Clinical Trials Regulation (CTR) No 536/2014, and all applicable industry regulations and
applicable national and local laws and regulations,” and “The clinical trial protocol, protocol
amendments, the investigator’s brochure, a sample ICF, and other trial-related documents
were reviewed and approved by the local or central IRBs/IECs of all trial sites.”
The Applicant has provided attestation that Study TAK-861-3002 was conducted in
accordance with GCP.
Financial Disclosure
The Applicant has adequately disclosed financial interests and arrangements with clinical
investigators. The Applicant did not disclose any interests or arrangements that raised
questions about the integrity of the study data.
Patient Disposition
A total of 140 individuals were screened for the trial, from whom 105 participants were
enrolled across 28 sites in Asia-Pacific, Australia, and Europe. Of the 35 screen failures, 32
participants did not meet the inclusion/exclusion criteria and three withdrew consent. All 105
enrolled participants were randomized to one of two groups: placebo (n=35) or oveporexton
2 mg BID (n=70). All enrolled participants received at least one dose of the trial intervention.
Of the 105 treated participants, 5 (4.8%) discontinued the trial. The reasons for
discontinuation are summarized by treatment group in the table below.

A total of 101 participants completed trial intervention, 96 (95%) entered the long-term
extension (LTE) trial immediately. Four additional participants (two in the oveporexton group,
two in the placebo group) entered the LTE trial with a dosing gap and were not captured in
the TAK-861-3002 electronic database. The total number entering the LTE trial was 100 of 101
(99%) participants who completed trial intervention. One participant completed trial
intervention and elected not to enter the LTE trial but did not attend the follow-up visits, so
he/she did not complete the trial.
In total, 100 participants completed the trial, representing 97.1% (34/35) of the placebo
group and 94.3% (66/70) of the oveporexton 2 mg BID group. A full summary of participant
disposition, including the number of participants (n) for each category, is provided in Table

The screen failure rate was 25.0% (35/140), which is reasonable for a trial with specific
eligibility criteria in a rare disease population. The majority of screen failures (91.4%; 32/35)
were due to not meeting inclusion/exclusion criteria, which does not raise concerns about the
representativeness of the enrolled population.
Few participants discontinued the overall program. A numerical imbalance in discontinuations
was observed between the treatment arms. The discontinuation rate was higher in the
oveporexton 2 mg BID group (5.7%; 4/70) compared to the placebo group (2.9%; 1/35).
This finding should be interpreted with caution. The interpretation of group difference is
limited by the small absolute number of discontinuation events in each group (n=1 in placebo,
n=4 in oveporexton). With such small event counts, a large difference in percentages can
arise due to random chance, and the analysis lacks the statistical power to support definitive
conclusions. Therefore, it is difficult to attribute clinical significance to this observed
imbalance based on the current data.
The specific AEs that led to these discontinuations are described in detail in the paragraphs
that follow.
Protocol Violations/Deviations
Participant-Level Deviations
Significant protocol deviations affected 61 of 105 participants (58.1%) in the randomized
sample. The overall proportion of participants with at least one deviation was higher in the
oveporexton group (61.4%) compared to the placebo group (51.4%).
Key Deviation Categories by Treatment Group:
•

Good Clinical Practice (GCP) Deviations (45.7% of all participants): This was the most
frequent category and was similar between the placebo (42.9%) and oveporexton (47.1%)
groups. The majority of these (n=46) involved breaches of participant confidentiality,
specifically "date of birth visible in ambulatory blood pressure monitoring (ABPM) report
due to system issue." Two participants had deviations in the subcategory of other GCP

179
Reference ID: 5847402

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 220860
Orzeyful (Oveporexton)
deviations (prior medication washout period started before the informed consent
document was signed).
•

Protocol Implementation Deviations (9.5% of all participants): These were more frequent in
the oveporexton group (11.4%) than the placebo group (5.7%). The most common
subcategory was "other (protocol implementation)" affecting 7 participants (6.7% overall).
Example of Other included: “Cataplexy e-Diary not completed for more than 7 days in a 2week period (except for Week 10 Week 12),” “Participant was not discontinued after they
met discontinuation criteria for elevated diastolic blood pressure,” and “Site gave dose past
D84 on EOT.”

•

Informed Consent Deviations (8.6% of all participants): These were balanced between the
placebo (8.6%) and oveporexton (8.6%) groups. The deviation for "consenting process was
missed" occurred in 6 participants total, with a higher concentration in the oveporexton
group (5 participants, 7.1%) compared to the placebo group (1 participant, 2.9%).

•

Eligibility Criteria Deviations (4.8% of all participants): Five participants were randomized
despite not meeting the study's eligibility criteria. All of these deviations occurred in the
oveporexton group (7.1%), with none reported in the placebo group.
- Oveporexton group: Two participants did not have an ESS score ≥11 at screening, one
participant had cataplexy eDiary compliance not met during screening period, one
participant had lack of PAP compliance prior to enrollment, and one participant did
not show to have a routine bedtime in the sleep diary.

•

Safety Deviations (2.9% of all participants): These occurred exclusively in the oveporexton
group (4.3%), with none reported in the placebo group. All three deviations involved
assessments (C-SSRS and CGI-S) performed by non-authorized staff.

•

Clinical Trial Material Deviations (1.0% of all participants): One participant in the
oveporexton group accidentally received an overdose of trial intervention.

•

Discontinuations Due to Protocol Deviations: No participants discontinued trial intervention
because of a protocol deviation.

At the trial level, five trial sites reported at least one significant site-level deviation. These
included:
•

GCP Deviations (three sites): Issues included participant identifier communicated to
Sponsor, CRO, vendor, and other vendor stipend deficiencies.

•

Protocol Implementation (two sites): Deviations involved failure to comply with IRB/IEC
requirements and lack of principal investigator’s oversight on trial.

Additionally, the Applicant identified additional protocol deviations after the database lock
(DBL) and submitted an erratum detailing these findings as follows:
Participant-Level Deviations
New Deviations Identified:
180
Reference ID: 5847402

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 220860
Orzeyful (Oveporexton)
Five new significant participant-level deviations were identified: three in the category of
safety deviations (non-qualified rater performed C-SSRS), and one each in the categories of
eligibility criteria (participant met exclusion criterion for elevated blood pressure) and
informed consent (optional DNA sample taken without obtaining consent).
In addition, five protocol deviations were reclassified following the database lock (DBL):
•

Three deviations were reclassified from nonsignificant to significant. All three deviations
were in the informed consent category and occurred because an optional DNA sample was
collected from a participant who had not signed the corresponding Informed Consent Form
(ICF).

•

Two deviations were reclassified from significant to nonsignificant. Both deviations were in
the protocol implementation category. One involved a missed visit at Early Termination, and
the other pertained to missing endpoint assessments at the Day 84 visit.

Site-Level Deviations
•

One new significant site-level deviation was identified in the category of protocol
implementation.

An analysis of protocol deviations revealed numerical imbalances between the treatment
arms in several key categories. Most notably, all eligibility criteria deviations occurred
exclusively in the oveporexton group. Prior to database lock, five such deviations were
identified, with an additional deviation found post-DBL, bringing the total to six participants
(8.6% of the oveporexton group) compared to zero in the placebo group.
An assessment of these six deviations shows they were heterogeneous in nature. While two
participants were enrolled who did not meet the ESS severity threshold, the other four
deviations were distinct, involving a lack of PAP compliance, failure to meet cataplexy eDiary
compliance, an irregular bedtime in the sleep diary, and meeting an exclusion criterion for
elevated blood pressure. This lack of a consistent pattern suggests isolated enrollment errors
rather than a systematic flaw that consistently skewed the study population toward being
more or less severe.
Additionally, all three safety deviations (assessments performed by non-authorized staff)
occurred in the oveporexton group. The GCP deviations, although the most frequent category
overall, were relatively balanced between groups (42.9% placebo versus 47.1% oveporexton)
and were primarily administrative in nature.
Given the varied nature of the eligibility deviations, it is unlikely they introduced a systematic
bias into the efficacy analysis. If these deviations had any collective impact, it would likely be
to underestimate the true treatment effect rather than artificially inflate it (i.e., recruitment
of less severe participants in the oveporexton group). Importantly, no participants
discontinued the trial intervention because of a protocol deviation, suggesting these issues
did not substantially impact participant conduct.
Ultimately, the observed differences are based on a very small number of participants.
Therefore, although these specific imbalances are noted for completeness, the varied nature
181
Reference ID: 5847402

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 220860
Orzeyful (Oveporexton)
and low frequency of these deviations make it improbable that they meaningfully altered the
interpretation of the trial's primary efficacy and safety outcomes. The Applicant's conclusion
that "participant-level protocol deviations did not affect the use and interpretation of the trial
data" appears reasonable.
Demographic Characteristics
Demographic characteristics were generally similar across the treatment groups (Table 66). Of
the 105 randomized participants, 45% were White and 20% were Asian. No Black participants
were enrolled. For 35% of participants, race was categorized as unknown, as these data were
not collected due to local regulations.
There was an imbalance in sex distribution between the groups. The placebo group was
predominantly male (62.9%), whereas the oveporexton group had a higher proportion of
females (52.9%). The median age of participants was 27.0 years, with a range of 18 to 62
years. No adolescent (<18 years) or older adult (≥65 years) participants were enrolled in the
study.
The study was conducted in Europe, Australia, and the Asia-Pacific region, with 80% of
participants enrolled in the Europe/Australia region.

182
Reference ID: 5847402

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 220860
Orzeyful (Oveporexton)
The demographic and baseline characteristics were generally similar across the placebo and
oveporexton treatment groups, though an imbalance in sex distribution was noted. There are
significant limitations regarding the age distribution, as the study population completely lacks
adolescent (<18) and older adult (≥65) participants. Therefore, the efficacy and safety of
oveporexton in these populations cannot be characterized from this study.
The generalizability of the findings to the United States population warrants consideration, as
the trial was conducted exclusively in Europe, Australia, and Asia, with no North American
sites. The complete absence of U.S. participants and the limited racial diversity are notable.
However, given that the underlying pathophysiology of NT1 does not differ across
populations, and given that the standard of care for diagnosis and treatment is similar in the
E.U., Australia, and the U.S., the concerns around generalizability are somewhat mitigated.
Additional reassurance is provided by data from TAK-861-3001, which enrolled 26 U.S.
participants, whose baseline demographics and NT1 disease severity were broadly
comparable to the non-U.S. population.
Furthermore, the Applicant conducted sensitivity analyses using a Bayesian hierarchical
model—implemented to address the limitation of small regional sample sizes—which showed
that treatment effects on MWT, ESS, and WCR in the North America subgroup were
consistent with the overall study population. Consistent with this finding, our own
exploratory subgroup analyses also showed a positive treatment effect in the U.S. population,
although only for the 2-mg BID dosage, as previously described in the TAK-861-3001 efficacy
results, although these analyses are of limited interpretability due to small sample size and
lack of prespecification.
Other Baseline Characteristics (e.g., Disease Characteristics, Important Concomitant Drugs)
The baseline disease characteristics of the participants were generally well-balanced across
the placebo and oveporexton treatment arms:
•

Disease History and Severity of Sleepiness: The treatment groups were highly comparable in
terms of disease history. The median age at symptom onset (15.0 years) and the median
age at NT1 diagnosis (21.6 years) were consistent across both arms. Furthermore, the key
baseline measures for EDS were severe and well-matched. The mean MWT sleep latency
was very low (around 4.5 minutes), and the mean ESS total score was very high (around 18),
indicating a population with a significant disease burden at study entry.

•

Baseline Cataplexy Rate: A numerical imbalance was noted in the baseline WCR. The mean
WCR was higher in the placebo group (45.8) compared to the oveporexton group (33.2).
However, the median values were more closely aligned (27 versus 22, respectively), and the
standard deviations were very large in both groups (around 40). This result suggests that
the difference in means is likely driven by a few participants with extremely high cataplexy
rates and may not represent a clinically significant systematic imbalance.

•

Diagnostic Confirmation and Prior Treatment: All participants had confirmed diagnoses of
NT1 through PSG/MSLT and either HLA positivity or documented historical CSF orexin levels
≤110 pg/mL, per the trial inclusion criteria. The proportion of participants who were HLA
183

Reference ID: 5847402

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 220860
Orzeyful (Oveporexton)
positive (approximately 98%) and had documented low CSF orexin levels (approximately
45%, combining participants with CSF reported in numeric or categorical values) were
similar across groups. The high rate of prior use of NT1 medications requiring washout
(approximately 87%) was also consistent across groups, indicating that the trial enrolled a
predominantly treatment-experienced population.
•

Lifestyle Factors: Baseline lifestyle characteristics such as alcohol and tobacco use were
similar across groups. A numerical imbalance was noted in caffeine consumption, with a
higher percentage of current users in the placebo group (68.6%) compared to the
oveporexton group (52.9%).

•

PAP Ventilation Use: The rate of PAP use for comorbid OSA was similar between groups
(5.7% in the placebo group versus 8.6% in the oveporexton group).

In conclusion, the treatment groups were highly comparable for most key parameters, with
minor numerical imbalances noted for baseline cataplexy rates and current caffeine use. A
careful consideration of each is warranted.
The higher rate of caffeine use in the placebo group could theoretically bias the results
against finding a significant effect for oveporexton, as caffeine could potentially improve
alertness and mask underlying sleepiness in the placebo arm. However, the protocol required
participants to be admitted to the clinical unit the day prior to key assessments like the MWT.
This controlled environment, which includes restrictions on substances like caffeine, provides
a robust layer of control that effectively mitigates any potential for acute caffeine effects to
confound these critical measurements.
The higher mean baseline WCR in the placebo group is a more complex issue. It is noteworthy
that a similar numerical imbalance in baseline WCR, with a higher mean in the placebo group,
was also observed in the pivotal Study TAK-861-3001. The recurrence of this imbalance across
two independent trials is likely a product of random chance combined with the high
variability of the WCR endpoint, rather than an indication of a systematic issue in trial
conduct. Theoretically, this imbalance could bias the results against finding a significant effect
for oveporexton. A group starting with a more extreme (higher) value has a greater potential
to show a large reduction, even by chance (i.e., regression to the mean). This difference could
artificially inflate the placebo response and, in turn, diminish the observed treatment
difference between oveporexton and placebo.
Therefore, despite these minor imbalances, their potential impact on the study's conclusions
is likely minimal due to mitigating factors in the trial's conduct. The treatment groups
remained highly comparable with respect to disease history, severity of sleepiness, and
diagnostic confirmation, again providing a solid basis for evaluating the treatment effects of
oveporexton.

184
Reference ID: 5847402

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 220860
Orzeyful (Oveporexton)
Treatment Compliance, Concomitant Medications, and Rescue Medication Use
Treatment Compliance
Overall treatment compliance was very high and comparable between groups, with a median
compliance rate of 99.4% in the oveporexton group and 98.8% in the placebo group. No
participants in either group had a compliance rate below 70%. A moderately higher
proportion of participants in the oveporexton group (20.0%, 14/70) had a compliance rate
over 100% compared to the placebo group (14.3%, 5/35), which may suggest some instances
of taking more than the prescribed dosage. An IR was issued to the Applicant for clarification.
As described for Study TAK-861-3001, the Applicant clarified that the above-threshold
compliance rates were attributable to reconciliation discrepancies during tablet
accountability, such as tablets being dropped or lost, or participants failing to return tablets
stored in a secondary container, rather than to actual excess dosing. No cases of confirmed
overdose were identified in Study TAK-861-3002.
Comorbidity
Concurrent medical conditions were frequently reported, affecting approximately 53% of all
participants, with a higher incidence in the placebo group (62.9%) than in the oveporexton
group (48.6%). Among the most common conditions, a higher rate in the placebo group was
noted for both Metabolism and nutrition disorders (17.1% versus 7.1% in the oveporexton
group) and Musculoskeletal and connective tissue disorders (14.3% versus 5.7%).
Two comorbidities of particular relevance to this trial, sleep apnea and psychiatric disorders,
were also examined. A slight numerical imbalance was observed for sleep apnea syndrome,
which was reported in 7.1% (5/70) of participants in the oveporexton group compared to
2.9% (1/35) in the placebo group. In contrast, the prevalence of psychiatric disorders was
found to higher in the placebo group, affecting 11.4% (4/35) of participants in the placebo
group versus 4.3% (3/70) in the oveporexton group.
Past and Concomitant Medications
The trial enrolled a heavily treatment-experienced population, with a high rate of prior
medication use for NT1 that was highly comparable between groups. Approximately 90% of
participants in the oveporexton group and 83% in the placebo group had a lifetime history of
using at least one NT1 therapy. A slight numerical imbalance was noted for lifetime use of
psychostimulants, which was reported in 78.6% of participants in the oveporexton group
versus 65.7% in the placebo group. Lifetime use of antidepressants, oxybate sodium, and
pitolisant was similar between groups.
The high level of active treatment prior to the study was further evidenced by the large
proportion of participants—approximately 81%—who required washout from their existing
NT1 medications (84% in the oveporexton group and 74% in the placebo group).
Regarding medication use during the trial, the rate of concomitant medication was
approximately 48% of participants across both groups (52.9% in the oveporexton group and
185
Reference ID: 5847402

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 220860
Orzeyful (Oveporexton)
37.1% in the placebo group). The most frequently reported therapeutic classes were Antiinflammatory and Antirheumatic Products, Non-Steroids, which were used at a similar rate in
the placebo (20%) and oveporexton (16%) groups.
The use of several prohibited medications was noted during the trial: modafinil, sodium
oxybate, and vortioxetine were each used by one participant in the oveporexton group, and
caffeine citrate was used by one participant in the placebo group. None of these cases were
reported as protocol deviations. An IR was issued to the Applicant for clarification. The
Applicant responded adequately, clarifying that modafinil and sodium oxybate were prior
medications incorrectly classified as concomitant due to date imputation and data entry
errors, respectively; that vortioxetine was approved by the Takeda medical monitor for
treatment of a depression adverse event and was not considered to have anticataleptic
properties; and that caffeine citrate use remained within the protocol-allowed limits and did
not overlap with key assessment days. Based on the Applicant's clarifications, none of these
cases constitute protocol deviations, and their potential impact on the trial results is
considered negligible.
Rescue Medication Use
No rescue medications were specified in the protocol.
A thorough review of baseline characteristics, comorbidities, and medication history confirms
that the treatment groups were generally well-balanced. Several minor numerical imbalances
were identified; however, these differences are based on small absolute numbers of
participants and are unlikely to have a clinically meaningful impact on the interpretation of
the efficacy and safety results.
Two trial conduct issues were identified and IRs were issued to the Applicant: the use of
prohibited concomitant medications by a small number of participants, and a higher
proportion of participants in the oveporexton group with compliance rates exceeding 100%.
The Applicant responded adequately to both IRs. The prohibited medication cases were
attributable to data entry and date imputation errors or were otherwise approved per
protocol procedures, and none constituted actual protocol deviations. The above-threshold
compliance rates were similarly explained by tablet accountability reconciliation
discrepancies rather than actual excess dosing. These matters are therefore considered
resolved and are not expected to have a meaningful impact on the interpretation of the
efficacy and safety results.
Overall, the initial randomization appears to have been successful, and the study arms are
considered highly comparable at baseline, providing a solid foundation for the evaluation of
oveporexton's treatment effect.
Efficacy Results–Primary Endpoint
Oveporexton 2 mg BID achieved a highly statistically significant improvement in the change
from Baseline to Week 12 in mean sleep latency on the MWT. The Least Square (LS) mean
changes and treatment differences in sleep latency on the MWT are displayed in Table 67.

higher in oveporexton 2 mg group than placebo (LS mean difference [95% CI] =20.09 [16.57,

Data Quality and Integrity
There were no data quality or integrity issues noted during the review.
Efficacy Results–Secondary and Other Relevant Endpoints
All secondary endpoints included in the multiple testing procedure were statistically
significant, yielding a very robust treatment effect. None of the results were sensitive to
missing data or modeling assumptions.
Oveporexton achieved a highly statistically significant improvements in the change from
Baseline to Week 12 in ESS total score, WCR, NSS-CT total score, mean number of lapses in
PVT, PGI-C Responder status at Week 12, FINI (all 6 domains of tiredness, cognitive
functioning, cataplexy, social activities, everyday activities, everyday responsibilities), and SF36. The results for all of these multiplicity protected endpoints are presented in Table 69.

Dose/Dose Response
Only one dosage was investigated in this study.
Durability of Response
As noted with Study 3001, effectiveness at 12 weeks likely indicates reasonable durability of
effect for oveporexton.

191
Reference ID: 5847402

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 220860
Orzeyful (Oveporexton)
Persistence of Effect
The Applicant did not report on persistence of effect; however, oveporexton and its PK profile
are intended for chronic daily dosing, and there is no expectation for a meaningful
persistence of effect after stopping oveporexton.
Efficacy Results–Additional or Exploratory COA (PRO) Endpoints
To investigate the effect of oveporexton on the entirety of the NT1 pentad (EDS, cataplexy,
hypnagogic/hypnopompic hallucinations, sleep paralysis, and disturbed/disrupted nighttime
sleep), exploratory endpoints may be used to examine the features not covered by the
multiplicity protected endpoints. Same as for Study TAK-861-3001, results for the pentad
symptoms of hypnagogic/hypnopompic hallucinations, sleep paralysis, and DNS are examined
using exploratory endpoints. Again, these analyses were not controlled for multiplicity, and as
such, need to be interpreted with caution. However, oveporexton appears to demonstrate a
treatment benefit for all of the NT1 pentad symptoms (Table 70).

Additional Analyses Conducted on the Individual Trial
In addition to the sensitivity analyses above, the Statistical Reviewer also conducted an
analysis focusing on study sites, to explore whether any one study site yielded results strongly
different from the rest of the study, or whether the efficacy results were primarily driven by a
single site. The results are presented in Figure 22, where the primary analysis is repeated,
leaving out one site at a time, with the results plotted against the site being left out. The
results were highly consistent and not dependent on any one site.
Figure 22. Sensitivity Analysis: Impact of Each Site on the Sleep Latency on the MWT Change
From Baseline Treatment Effect vs. Placebo at Week 12 in the Full Analysis Set (Study TAK861-3002)

Source: Statistical Reviewer

Integrated Review of Effectiveness
Similar to the conclusion for Study TAK-861-3001, for Study TAK-861-3002 the review team
confirms the Applicant’s primary finding that the 2-mg BID dosage of oveporexton is superior
compared with placebo for the primary endpoint of change from Baseline to Week 12 in
mean sleep latency on the MWT. The study yielded highly statistically significant results on
the primary endpoint and all secondary endpoints included in the multiple testing procedure.
The results were robust to missing data or modeling choices due to large magnitude of effect
and low amount of missing data. In addition, exploratory endpoints that evaluate other
aspects of the NT1 symptom pentad compared to the endpoints included in the multiple
testing procedure also generally supported the finding that oveporexton yielded an effect on
all of the symptoms in the pentad.
194
Reference ID: 5847402

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 220860
Orzeyful (Oveporexton)
As with Study TAK-861-3001, the Applicant is seeking an indication for NT1 as a whole,
requiring demonstration of a meaningful treatment effect across all five core components of
the NT1 pentad (EDS, cataplexy, hypnagogic/hypnopompic hallucinations, sleep paralysis, and
DNS). The efficacy results from Study TAK-861-3002, which evaluated oveporexton 2 mg BID
only, are reviewed below through the lens of each pentad symptom.
•

Excessive Daytime Sleepiness (EDS): The evidence supporting an effect of oveporexton 2 mg
BID on EDS is robust and consistent with Study TAK-861-3001. Oveporexton 2 mg BID
achieved a highly statistically significant improvement on the MWT, with a magnitude of
effect numerically larger than that observed in Study TAK-861-3001. A highly statistically
significant improvement was similarly observed on the ESS, confirming concordance
between objective and subjective measures of EDS.

•

Cataplexy: Oveporexton 2 mg BID demonstrated a robust and statistically significant
reduction in weekly cataplexy rate relative to placebo—a numerically greater effect than
observed in Study TAK-861-3001. This result provides strong and consistent evidence of a
clinically meaningful treatment effect on cataplexy, the pathognomonic feature of NT1.

•

Hypnagogic/Hypnopompic Hallucinations: Oveporexton 2 mg BID showed nominally
significant reductions in hallucination frequency and bother, both in the overall population
and in the subgroup of participants with at least one hallucination episode per week at
baseline. The magnitude of effect in the symptomatic subgroup was numerically larger than
that observed in Study TAK-861-3001, further supporting a meaningful treatment effect on
this pentad symptom.

•

Sleep Paralysis: Oveporexton 2 mg BID showed nominally significant reductions in sleep
paralysis, both in the overall population and in the subgroup of participants with at least
one sleep paralysis episode per week at Baseline. These results are consistent with and
confirmatory of the findings from Study TAK-861-3001.

•

Disturbed/Disrupted Nighttime Sleep (DNS): As in Study TAK-861-3001, the DNS domain
presents a nuanced picture. Positive and nominally significant results were observed for
multiple DNS-related measures, including subjective sleep quality items, nPSG-measured
REM latency, nights per week of good or excellent sleep quality, and the SF-36 Vitality
domain. As observed in Study TAK-861-3001, nPSG-measured nocturnal awakenings
showed a numerical increase with oveporexton relative to placebo, though this did not
reach nominal significance in Study TAK-861-3002. The clinical review team reiterates the
interpretation offered in the context of Study TAK-861-3001: the increase in objectively
measured nocturnal awakenings is not unexpected given the wake-promoting mechanism
of action of oveporexton and should not be interpreted in isolation as evidence of a
detrimental effect on nighttime sleep. The nominally significant improvement in nPSGmeasured REM latency—replicated across both pivotal studies with a highly consistent
magnitude of effect—provides compelling objective evidence that oveporexton restores
REM sleep regulation, which is the core biological abnormality underlying DNS,
hallucinations, and sleep paralysis in NT1. Taken together with the nominally significant

195
Reference ID: 5847402

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 220860
Orzeyful (Oveporexton)
improvements in subjective sleep quality measures, the clinical review team considers the
DNS-related evidence supportive of a treatment benefit on this pentad domain.
Taken together, the efficacy data from Study TAK-861-3002 provide consistent and
confirmatory evidence of a treatment effect across all five components of the NT1 pentad.
The results are highly concordant with those from Study TAK-861-3001, with comparable or
numerically larger effect sizes across all evaluated endpoints. This clinical reviewer considers
the evidence from Study TAK-861-3002 further supportive of the Applicant's approach to the
NT1 indication.
Study TAK-861-2001
8.1.3.1.

Study Design

Study TAK-861-2001 was a phase 2 dose-ranging study designed to inform dosage selection
and further development of TAK-861 as a potential treatment for NT1. The study was a
randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled, multicenter trial designed to evaluate the
efficacy, safety, and tolerability of different dosages of TAK-861 in adults with NT1.
Participants were randomized across 33 sites in the United States, Europe, Australia, and
Japan to one of the following five treatment arms over an 8-week treatment period:
•

TAK-861 Dose Regimen 1: 0.5 mg twice daily approximately 3 hours apart

•

TAK-861 Dose Regimen 2: 2 mg twice daily approximately 3 hours apart

•

TAK-861 Dose Regimen 3: 2 mg followed by 5 mg approximately 3 hours apart

•

TAK-861 Dose Regimen 4: 7 mg once daily (QD)

•

Matching placebo

After the Week 8 visit, participants had the option to participate in the LTE TAK-861-2003,
similar to the phase 3 studies previously described.
Participants were adults with an ICSD-3 diagnosis of NT1 confirmed by PSG or MSLT, and ≥4
partial and/or complete episodes of cataplexy per week, with inclusion criteria similar to
those of the phase 3 studies.
The primary endpoint was change from Baseline to Week 8 in mean sleep latency on the
MWT, with secondary endpoints including change from Baseline to Week 8 in ESS total score
and WCR at Week 8. Exploratory endpoints included, among others, change from Baseline in
NSS-CT and change from Baseline in various nPSG measures.
Based on the results of this study, the Applicant designed and selected the dosages for the
phase 3 pivotal studies previously described.
8.1.3.2.

Study Results

A total of 112 participants were randomized, of which 22% (n=25) were from the United
States. The Full Analysis Set included all participants who were randomized, received at least
one dose of study drug, and had at least one postdose efficacy measurement (N=112).

Only two of the four regimens (2 mg BID and 2 mg/5 mg) significantly reduced WCR (adjusted p<0.05; Table 73). At Week 8, the
estimated incidence rate of weekly cataplexy episodes was 8.76 (95% CI: 5.68, 13.51) in the placebo group, compared to 4.24,

adjusted p =0.250) groups did not reach statistical significance after multiplicity adjustment.

Reference ID: 5847402

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 220860
Orzeyful (Oveporexton)
At Week 8, all four TAK-861 dosage regimens reduced ESS total scores from baseline
compared to placebo (adjusted p =0.004 for the 0.5 mg BID group; adjusted p<0.001 for all
other groups). The LS mean change from Baseline in the placebo group was -2.50 (SE 1.109),
compared to -8.92 (SE 1.085), -13.79 (SE 1.115), -12.81 (SE 1.073), and -11.29 (SE 1.064) in the
TAK-861 0.5 mg BID, 2 mg BID, 2 mg/5 mg, and 7 mg QD groups, respectively. The
corresponding LS mean differences from placebo ranged from -6.42 (95% CI: -9.53, -3.32) for
the 0.5 mg BID group to -11.30 (95% CI: -14.44, -8.16) for the 2 mg BID group, with the two
higher BID regimens showing the largest reductions.
At Week 8, all four TAK-861 dosage regimens reduced NSS-CT total scores from baseline
compared to placebo (p<0.001 for all groups). The LS mean change from Baseline in the
placebo group was -3.50 (SE 1.787), compared to -18.24 (SE 1.767), -21.03 (SE 1.812), -21.14
(SE 1.748), and -17.23 (SE 1.745) in the TAK-861 0.5 mg BID, 2 mg BID, 2 mg/5 mg, and 7 mg
QD groups, respectively. The corresponding LS mean differences from placebo ranged from 13.73 (95% CI: -18.65, -8.81) for the 7 mg QD group to -17.64 (95% CI: -22.62, -12.66) for the
2 mg/5 mg group, with broadly similar reductions observed across the three higher-dosage
regimens.
Taken together, the results of Study TAK-861-2001 suggest that TAK-861 reduced excessive
daytime sleepiness, cataplexy frequency, and narcolepsy symptom severity across all dosage
regimens tested, providing early evidence of efficacy across the core symptoms of NT1.
However, the pattern of results across dosages was not straightforward: while all four
regimens showed statistically significant improvements in EDS (MWT and ESS) and narcolepsy
symptom severity (NSS-CT), only the two intermediate BID regimens (2 mg BID and
2 mg/5 mg) achieved statistically significant reductions in WCR after multiplicity adjustment.
The lowest dosage (0.5 mg BID) and the once-daily regimen (7 mg QD) did not demonstrate
significant cataplexy reduction, suggesting that dose level and regimen may differentially
affect NT1 symptom domains. These findings informed the Applicant's decision to evaluate
dosages of 1 mg BID and 2 mg BID in the phase 3 pivotal studies, with the aim of identifying a
dosage range that balances efficacy across different endpoints with an acceptable safety and
tolerability profile.
Assessment of Efficacy Across Trials: Integrated Assessment of
Effectiveness
The substantial evidence of effectiveness for this NDA is based on the phase 3 Studies TAK861-3001 and TAK-861-3002. The phase 2 Study TAK-861-2001 was also an adequate and
well-controlled study that provided additional support for the phase 3 findings; however, it
was not required for a determination of substantial evidence of effectiveness and was,
therefore, not subject to independent reanalysis. Its results are summarized in this review for
completeness. The positive efficacy results reported by the Applicant for TAK-861-2001 are
consistent with the findings from TAK-861-3001 and TAK-861-3002. Note that efficacy results
were not pooled.

Orzeyful (Oveporexton)

Review of Safety
Safety Review Approach
This safety review focused primarily on the two phase 3 safety and efficacy studies in the NT1
population that compared oveporexton to placebo at the to-be-marketed doses—TAK-8613001 and TAK-861-3002, pooled together, as they were of similar dosing, design, and length.
These studies were also used for the efficacy analysis. See Section 8.1 for a detailed
description of the study designs and characteristics.
The clinical reviewer assessed study reports, AE database, laboratory database,
electrocardiogram database, vital sign database, and participants' scores on the C-SSRS for
each study separately and pooled together. Based on the mechanism of action and previous
AEs associated with drugs in the same class, elevated liver function tests (LFTs), urinary
events, blood pressure (BP) and heart rate (HR) increases, and insomnia were considered
adverse events of special interest (AESI). Finally, given psychiatric comorbidities that often
accompany narcolepsy and the product's effects on the central nervous system (CNS),
psychiatric AEs were also examined closely, especially the risk for suicidal ideation and
behavior (SI/B).
The clinical reviewer also conducted a review of AEs in the long-term safety study TAK-8612003, including the updated 90-day safety report.
In addition, the clinical reviewer reviewed the Applicant's integrated summary of safety (ISS)
database, which includes data from additional narcolepsy trials as well as trials of
oveporexton in healthy volunteers, for serious AEs. The most recent Periodic Benefit Risk
Evaluation Report was also reviewed.

The Applicant presented safety data grouping studies in different ways:
•

All participants pool includes all participants from all trials (see Table 51 for a list of all
studies; dosages 0.5 mg/day to 75 mg/day)

•

Healthy participant (HP) pool includes HP from phase 1 trials (TAK-861-1001 (parts A, B, C,
E, F), TAK-861-1002, TAK-861-1003, TAK-861-1004, TAK-861-1005, TAK-861-1007, TAK-8611010, TAK-861-1011; dosages 1 mg/day to 75 mg/day)

•

NT1 Long Term Extension (LTE) Pool includes participants with NT1 from LTE trial including
their phase 2 and 3 parent trial data (TAK-861-2001, TAK-861-2003, TAK-861-3001, TAK861-3002; dosages 0.5 mg/day to 8 mg/day)

•

NT1 Placebo-Controlled (PC) Pool includes participants with NT1 from phase 1, phase 2, and
phase 3 double-blind placebo-controlled trials (TAK-861-1001 (parts D, G), TAK-861-2001,
TAK-861-3001, TAK-861-3002; dosages 0.5 mg/day to 8 mg/day)

•

Pivotal Trial Pool includes participants with NT1 from phase 3 registrational trials (TAK-8613001 and TAK-861-3002; dosages 2 mg/day to 4 mg/day)

The heterogeneity of study designs, populations, and narcolepsy disease type (i.e., NT1 and
NT2) limited the use of the Applicant’s full ISS database (i.e., All participant pool) for analysis
of safety signals for the narcolepsy indication. However, the Applicant’s full ISS database was
reviewed for deaths and serious adverse events.
This safety review focused on pooled data from the Pivotal Trial Pool (i.e., the two
registrational studies) given the similarity in designs and populations and given that these two
studies are the only placebo-controlled trials to include the to-be-marketed doses. The
demographic and other baseline characteristics for participants in Study TAK-861-3001 and
TAK-861-3002 were previously discussed (see relevant Sections 8.1.1.1 and 8.1.2.1).
Regarding exposure, the Agency may consider regulatory discretion in applying ICH E1
exposure guidelines because of oveporexton's Orphan Drug Designation for NT1, given the
inherent challenges in achieving the recommended exposure numbers in rare disease
populations. The 90-day safety update demonstrates that exposure totals meet ICH E1
requirements for both of the longer-term 6-month and 12-month durations (i.e., at least 300
participants with 6-month exposure and 100 participants with 12-month exposure).
For short-term exposure, although 823 subjects exposed is lower than the ICH-recommended
1500—given the unmet medical need for NT1 (and given the overall benefit-risk profile
characterized in this review), we consider these exposure numbers acceptable to adequately
characterize the safety of the drug to support an initial approval.
Furthermore, exposure to the maximum to-be-marketed dose (4 mg) is adequate, with 311
participants exposed to dosages ≥4 mg total daily dosage, representing approximately 40% of
the total exposed population (311 out of 770 unique participants exposed to oveporexton).
Regarding baseline characteristics, the demographic results from the registrational studies
are sufficiently generalizable for safety assessment purposes, consistent with previous
comments on demographic characteristics and the use of foreign data.

Orzeyful (Oveporexton)
Adequacy of Applicant’s Clinical Safety Assessments
Issues Regarding Data Integrity and Submission Quality
This submission contains all required components of the eCTD. The overall quality and
integrity of the datasets appear acceptable.
Categorization of Adverse Events
The Applicant categorized adverse events using Medical Dictionary for Regulatory Activities
(MedDRA) version 28.0. The Applicant used standard methods for severity coding and
definitions of serious adverse events (SAEs) and AEs leading to discontinuation. A TEAE was
defined as "any event emerging or manifesting at or after the initiation of treatment with a
study intervention or medicinal product or any existing event that worsens in either intensity
or frequency following exposure to the study intervention or medicinal product." The terms
"AE" and "TEAE" are used interchangeably throughout this document and should be
understood to refer to TEAEs as defined above.
AEs of special interest (AESIs) were events of scientific and medical concern specific to
oveporexton that required ongoing monitoring and rapid communication, based on
nonclinical data, clinical data from compounds with similar mechanisms of action, and
literature on OX2R agonism effects. All AESIs were recorded in the eCRF and followed until
resolution, with specific monitoring protocols established for blood pressure/heart rate and
liver function tests throughout the studies. The four AESI categories identified for the
oveporexton studies were:
•

Urinary Events, including pollakiuria, micturition urgency, enuresis, dysuria, bladder
discomfort, urinary incontinence, nocturia, urinary hesitation, and urine flow decreased

•

Insomnia, including insomnia, initial insomnia, and middle insomnia

•

Elevated Liver Function Tests, including alanine aminotransferase increased, aspartate
aminotransferase increased, gamma-glutamyltransferase increased, hepatic enzyme
increased, hepatic steatosis, hepatic function abnormal, liver function test abnormal, liver
function test increased, metabolic dysfunction-associated steatohepatitis, and
transaminases increased

•

BP and HR Increases, including palpitations, blood pressure increased, heart rate increased,
blood pressure diastolic increased, and hypertension

The NDA contained narratives for deaths and SAEs that occurred in the overall oveporexton
clinical development program. The Applicant submitted case narratives for AEs leading to
discontinuations and of serious AESIs in response to an Agency's IRs during the NDA review
cycle.
The Applicant’s SAE, AE severity, and TEAE definitions appear reasonable. On examination of
TEAEs, no further AESI grouping appeared necessary to capture relevant AE concepts. Upon
review of the AE databases for the two registrational studies, it appears that AEs were coded
207
Reference ID: 5847402

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 220860
Orzeyful (Oveporexton)
appropriately, and the Applicant's mapping of verbatim and preferred terms (PTs) was
acceptable. The AE assessment strategies employed in the clinical development program
were adequate and appropriate.
Routine Clinical Tests
See Table 53 and Table 62 for detailed schedules of assessments for studies TAK-861-3001
and TAK-861-3002.
Safety and tolerability were evaluated using reported AEs, clinical laboratory evaluations
(including creatine kinase and liver function tests), pregnancy testing, vital signs, ECGs, ABPM,
physical examination findings, and SI/B assessment. (See study protocols for more specific
details.)
The C-SSRS was used to assess events of suicidal ideation and behavior at all visits. C-SSRS is a
standard questionnaire (Screening/Baseline and Since Last Visit) used to assess treatmentemergent suicidal ideation and behavior in clinical studies. A “Yes” answer at any time to any
one of the five suicidal ideation and associated behavioral questions on the C-SSRS Since Last
Visit, was indicative of possible suicidal risk. The event was recorded as an AE. The
Investigator ensured that any suicidal ideation or behavior was medically addressed, including
assessment and treatment by qualified medical personnel. In addition, if the subject
answered “yes” to question 4 or 5, the study drug should have been discontinued. If the
subject answered “yes” to question 1 or 2 or 3, the mental health professional should have
determined whether the study drug should have been discontinued or not. The C-SSRS was
administered at least every 4 weeks, and during the first follow-up visit in the registrational
studies.
Specific to the long-term extension study (TAK-861-2003), there was the modified
International Prostate Symptom Score (mIPSS), an IPSS version without the quality of life
(QoL) question. This scale is used to characterize the frequency and severity of lower urinary
tract symptoms. It consists of seven questions to self-assess urinary symptoms of 1)
incomplete emptying, 2) frequency, 3) intermittency, 4) urgency, 5) weak stream, 6) straining,
and 7) nocturia. Total scores of 0 to 7, 8 to 19, and 20 to 35 are graded as mild, moderate,
and severe lower urinary tract symptoms, respectively. The mIPSS with 7-day recall in the
long-term extension study was administered to all participants at Day 1 (Baseline) and Weeks
2, 4, 12, 26, 38, 52, and 104.
To summarize, the clinical laboratory assessments, additional safety assessments, and
collection schedule for laboratory assessments, vital signs, ECGs, and other assessments were
generally reasonable. Follow-up at 7 and 28 days after the last dose appears an adequate
timeframe to collect AEs that may arise after drug discontinuation. Blood chemistry
assessments at 28 days after dose discontinuation also allowed for an adequate assessment
of abnormal levels in laboratory parameters, including liver function tests. Threshold Levels 1,
2, and 3 are defined by the Standard Safety Tables & Figures Integrated Guide (Abnormality
Level Criteria).

208
Reference ID: 5847402

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 220860
Orzeyful (Oveporexton)
The inclusion of the C-SSRS administered at least every 4 weeks and during the first follow-up
visit was appropriate for monitoring suicidal ideation and behavior risk in this population.
Additionally, the use of accredited central laboratories ensured consistency of analyses and
reporting of results for clinical laboratory tests. The incorporation of the mIPSS in the longterm extension study (TAK-861-2003) was a reasonable approach to systematically monitor
lower urinary tract symptoms given the known pharmacological effects of oveporexton on
urinary function.
The DILI and DUOG teams were consulted during protocol review. An LSEC (Liver Safety
Evaluation Committee), independent of the oveporexton clinical program and DMC,
"reviewed any cases in which there was concern for possible DILI, including all cases of
alkaline phosphatase elevations >2×ULN, trial intervention discontinuations due to liverrelated AEs, and liver-related SAEs." For detailed assessment of hepatic safety issues, refer to
the DILI team review. For evaluation of urological safety concerns, including urinary adverse
events and mIPSS findings, refer to the DUOG review.
Long-Term Extension Safety Study TAK-861-2003
Study Title
Study TAK-861-2003 is titled "A Long-term Extension Study to Evaluate the Safety and
Tolerability of TAK-861 in Participants With Selected Central Hypersomnia Conditions."
Objectives
The study's primary objective was to evaluate the long-term safety and tolerability of
oveporexton, as measured by the occurrence of at least one treatment-emergent adverse
event (TEAE). Secondary objectives included assessment of the effect of oveporexton on:
•

Changes in EDS as measured by the mean sleep latency from the MWT

•

Changes in EDS as measured by the ESS total score

•

Changes in cataplexy episodes as measured by the WCR.

Study Design Overview
Study TAK-861-2003 is an ongoing phase 2/3, multicenter, dose-blind, long-term extension
(LTE) trial designed to evaluate the long-term safety and tolerability of oveporexton in
participants with NT1 who completed a parent trial. The planned trial period was
approximately 5 years, or until the trial is stopped at the Sponsor's discretion, or until the
product is approved and launched. This interim Clinical Study Report (CSR) contains data as of
the data cutoff date of August 29, 2025. A 90-day safety report contains data as of the data
cutoff date of November 30, 2025.
Participants who rolled over directly into the LTE from a parent trial with a dosing gap of ≤7
days had their eligibility assessed during the final visit of the parent trial. Participants entering
the LTE with a dosing gap of >7 days from the end date of their parent trial underwent a
209
Reference ID: 5847402

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 220860
Orzeyful (Oveporexton)
screening period of up to 4 weeks, during which they completed washout of narcolepsy
medications and other restricted medications.
Day 1 dosing for all participants occurred in-clinic with PK and safety assessments. Clinic visits
after Week 4 were scheduled monthly up to Week 12, then approximately every 3 months
until the end of the trial. The higher in-clinic visit frequency during the first 12 weeks ensured
appropriate safety monitoring for participants who were previously on placebo and/or had a
dosing gap. Additional visits could be conducted through a remote visit, such as completing a
local/nearby medical laboratory draw or conducting a home visit.
Laboratory tests for safety were obtained biweekly up until Week 12, followed by monthly
until Month 12, and then approximately every 3 months thereafter, as well as 4 weeks after
the last dose. Participants who underwent nPSG and an MWT during the final assessment of
their parent trial had the option of staying overnight in the clinic on Day -1 of the LTE.
Participants stayed overnight at the clinic on Day 181 and were discharged on Day 182.
Trial Location
This multi-center study was conducted at 93 sites across three geographic regions: AsiaPacific, Europe, and North America. The parent trials that rolled over into the LTE included
TAK-861-2001, TAK-861-3001, TAK-861-3002, and TAK-861-1001 Part G. Participants from
TAK-861-2002 (only NT2) also rolled over into this LTE; however, because TAK-861-2002 did
not demonstrate clinical benefit, these participants were subsequently discontinued. The
interim CSR submitted by the Applicant to the NDA did not include data on this participant
population.
Diagnostic Criteria
Participants enrolled in Study TAK-861-2003 were required to have completed a controlled
trial with oveporexton and to have a diagnosis of NT1 (or NT2 for participants from TAK-8612002, who were subsequently discontinued from the LTE following the demonstration of lack
of efficacy in the parent trial). The diagnostic criteria for NT1 were those applied in the
respective parent trials, based on ICSD-3 or ICSD-3-TR criteria, as described in Section 8.1.1
(Study TAK-861-3001). The interim CSR submitted by the Applicant to the NDA included data
only for participants with NT1.
Key Eligibility Criteria
Key inclusion criteria include that participants must have completed a controlled trial with
oveporexton and have no clinical objection from the investigator to enrollment into the LTE.
•

A dosing gap of up to 3 months was allowed between the parent trial and the screening for
the LTE. Additionally, participants from TAK-861-1001 Part G could be enrolled regardless of
the length of the dosing gap.

•

Participants entering the LTE with a dosing gap of ≤7 days after the end of the parent trial
followed the same rules as participants entering the trial without a dosing gap.
210

Reference ID: 5847402

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 220860
Orzeyful (Oveporexton)
Key exclusion criteria include:
•

Any participant in a parent trial with an AE related to trial intervention that remained
severe at the time of rollover was excluded from the LTE.

•

Participants with a dosing gap >7 days were excluded if they had screening ALT or AST
values >1.5 times the upper limit of normal (ULN) or GGT >3 times the ULN, and were
required to have at least one AST and ALT result available from ≤16 days before the first
dose.

•

Participants with urinary conditions that would preclude enrollment, per the investigator's
judgment.

•

Participants unable to refrain from prohibited medications, including known moderate or
strong cytochrome P450 type 3A inhibitors or inducers, and drugs used for the treatment of
narcolepsy.

Subject Completion, Dose Discontinuation, and Withdrawal
The trial is ongoing. Participants can discontinue from the trial for the following reasons:
voluntary withdrawal of consent, loss to follow-up, pregnancy, major protocol deviations, or
appearance of an AE that would impose an unacceptable risk to the participant's health if
participation continued. Participants who discontinued study drug were asked to remain in
the study for all protocol-specified visits. Blood chemistry was required at approximately 4
weeks after the last dose.
Safety Results
Deaths
The Applicant submitted narrative summaries for all deaths that occurred in the clinical
development program in the NDA and submitted an update in the 90-day safety report.
Pivotal Trial Pool
There were no deaths in Study TAK-861-3001 and Study TAK-861-3002.
Other Integrated Summary of Safety Studies
One death occurred in the oveporexton development program, in the LTE Study TAK-8612003:
Participant
: A 52-year-old male participant receiving oveporexton 2 mg BID
experienced a fatal AE of cardiac arrest. The participant had a medical history of hypertension,
hyperlipidemia, class 2 obesity, and OSA. The cardiac arrest occurred on Day 480,
approximately 18 months after treatment initiation, including exposure during the parent trial.
There were no reported symptoms or signs of distress or foul play. The event was assessed by
the investigator as not related to the trial intervention.
(b) (6)

211
Reference ID: 5847402

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 220860
Orzeyful (Oveporexton)
Although this patient had comorbid health conditions (OSA, hyperlipidemia, untreated
hypertension) that could have contributed to his risk of death, the data are insufficient to
conclude whether the death was related to oveporexton. The presence of multiple
cardiovascular risk factors in this participant raised important questions regarding baseline
cardiovascular risk stratification and whether the Applicant appropriately followed the
protocol's exclusion criteria. Specifically, the protocol defined exclusion criteria included
"apnea-hypopnea index at or greater than 15 on Day -2 nPSG" and participants with preexisting hypertension were defined as those with "baseline SBP >=130 mm Hg, baseline DBP
>=80 mm Hg, or medical history of hypertension."
Notably, while the summary of clinical safety report indicated that the participant had a
"medical history of untreated hypertension," this critical information was not included in the
individual patient narrative provided in TAK-861-2003 CSR, Section 15.3.3.1.3, or other critical
information necessary for a complete assessment of this fatal event, such as whether the
participant met all eligibility criteria.
The Applicant responded to an Information Request (IR) with a detailed account of the
participant's eligibility at enrollment. Regarding blood pressure, the borderline elevated BP
recorded at the initial screening visit (143/89 mm Hg) was attributed by the investigator to
concomitant use of solriamfetol and situational stress. A confirmatory Day -2 measurement of
123/86 mm Hg was below the protocol exclusion threshold, and BP remained generally stable
throughout both trials, with no values meeting pre-specified discontinuation criteria. AHI
values at all relevant time points were below the exclusionary threshold, and no evidence of
clinically significant coronary artery disease, cardiac rhythm abnormality, or heart failure was
present at baseline. The Applicant further clarified that the characterization of "untreated
hypertension" in the Summary of Clinical Safety report was based on BP elevations observed
during the trial, not on a documented diagnosis at enrollment.
Based on the above, the participant appears to have met all protocol-defined eligibility
criteria, and the investigator's judgment regarding the borderline screening BP appears
adequately justified. The final determination as to the death event’s association with the drug
remains uncertain, although the aforementioned background cardiovascular risk factors may
have contributed.

The Applicant submitted narrative summaries for all SAEs that occurred in the clinical

Pivotal Trial Pool
In the pivotal trial pool, treatment-emergent SAEs occurred only in the overall oveporexton
group (2/196 participants [1.0%] compared to 0/76 participants in the placebo group). Both
SAEs occurred in Study TAK-861-3001:
•

Participant
: A 23-year-old male receiving oveporexton 1 mg BID experienced an
SAE of ureterolithiasis (kidney stone in the left ureter) on Day 79. The event required

In the LTE trial (TAK-861-2003), in addition to the fatal SAE described previously, there were

cannot exclude the possibility that oveporexton may have exacerbated the underlying

90-Day Safety Update
This 90-day safety update presents updated data from the only ongoing trial (LTE trial TAK861-2003) and lists all participants who experienced an SAE between August 29, 2025, and
November 30, 2025.
There were two additional SAEs in the 90-day safety update:
•

Participant
: A 23-year-old male receiving oveporexton experienced an SAE of
severe acute myeloid leukemia (seriousness criterion: hospitalization) 5 months and 10
days after starting oveporexton. The study drug was interrupted, and the subject withdrew
from the study. The investigator assessed the event as not related to the blinded study
drug. There is very limited information available.

•

Participant
: A 25-year-old female receiving oveporexton experienced an SAE of
multiple fractures (right ankle and right navicular) after falling from an electric bicycle
(which, per the report, was not caused by narcolepsy) 6 months and 9 days after starting
oveporexton and required hospitalization. The drug was continued. The investigator
assessed the event as not related to the study drug.

(b) (6)

(b) (6)

The two SAEs reported in the 90-day safety update are consistent with the overall safety
profile observed in the oveporexton development program. The acute myeloid leukemia case
(b) (6)
(Participant
) occurred after 5 months of exposure with very limited information
available; given the very limited information available, a causal relationship with oveporexton
cannot be definitively established or excluded at this time, but it is unlikely given there is no
known mechanistic association between orexin receptor agonism and hematologic
malignancies. This was discussed with the non-clinical team, the Division of Epidemiology, and
the Division of Oncology. (See Section 8.2.8.1 Subsection Human Carcinogenicity or Tumor
Development for more details.)
The multiple fractures case (Participant
) resulted from a clear traumatic etiology
(fall from electric bicycle) that was explicitly denied to be related to narcolepsy symptoms,
and occurred in the placebo arm, raising no safety concerns.
(b) (6)

In summary, across the oveporexton development program, the incidence of SAEs was low in
placebo-controlled studies (pivotal trial pool: 1.0% oveporexton versus 0% placebo) and in
the long-term extension (2.2% overall in the NT1 LTE pool). All reported SAEs appeared
unrelated to study drug, with clear alternative etiologies identified in each case. Notably, the
majority of musculoskeletal injury-related SAEs (joint injury, tendon rupture, ankle fracture,
and multiple fractures) occurred in the LTE trial, where participants were not receiving
placebo, making direct comparison with a placebo-control group not feasible. While these
events had clear traumatic etiologies associated with physical activities or sports, it is
215
Reference ID: 5847402

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 220860
Orzeyful (Oveporexton)
theoretically possible that participants treated with oveporexton, experiencing improved
wakefulness and reduced excessive daytime sleepiness, may have engaged in more physical
activity and thus had increased exposure to situations where musculoskeletal injuries could
occur. However, this remains a hypothesis, and the absence of a concurrent placebo control
in the LTE trial limits the ability to establish any causal relationship with the drug. The SAE
profile does not reveal any drug-related safety signal or pattern of concern.

Placebo group:
•

Participant
(TAK-861-3001): A 31-year-old female receiving placebo, experienced
LFT increase ((ALT 3.4x ULN, AST 1.5x ULN, ALP 1x ULN; all other LFTs WNL) on Day 14. On

(b) (6)

The discontinuation rate due to adverse events appeared numerically higher in the
oveporexton group (2.6%) compared to placebo (1.3%) in the pivotal trials; however, the
absolute number of events was limited, making the clinical significance of this difference
unclear.
Two cases of PT “rhabdomyolysis” occurred in participants receiving oveporexton 2 mg BID,
both associated with intense physical exercise. These cases were characterized primarily by
laboratory findings (elevated CK, AST, and ALT meeting protocol discontinuation criteria)
rather than clinical symptoms. Notably, neither case presented with the classic triad of
rhabdomyolysis—there was no dark (tea- or cola-colored) urine, no myalgia, and no muscle
weakness—and therefore, despite meeting the PT definition by laboratory criteria, these
cases may not represent true clinical rhabdomyolysis. Both participants were asymptomatic
or minimally symptomatic, neither developed RI, and no medical intervention was required.
One case was assessed as related to the combination of intense sport and trial intervention
(Gilbert's syndrome was suspected per hepatologist assessment), while the other was
considered unrelated and attributed solely to exercise, resolving within 2 weeks. These
findings suggest exercise-induced rhabdomyolysis potentially amplified by oveporexton—a
manageable risk with appropriate patient counseling.
The two cases of rhabdomyolysis due to excessive exercise included elevated liver function
tests (LFTs). A third case of LFT elevation had an alternative etiology: suspected infectious
mononucleosis (positive EBV/CMV serology with lymphadenitis). No cases met Hy's Law
criteria, and the overall incidence of elevated LFTs leading to discontinuation remained low
and comparable between groups (1.5% oveporexton versus 1.3% placebo).
Two urinary event discontinuations (pollakiuria and urinary incontinence) occurred in the
oveporexton 1 mg BID group and related to treatment. These represent on-target
pharmacological effects. Discontinuations were rare (1.0%), and most events were mild-tomoderate, starting within 2 days and resolving by trial end.
Product labeling should address increased CPK risk with recommendations for patients to
report unexplained muscle symptoms.
Patient counseling about expected urinary effects and their transient nature may reduce
discontinuations.

Orzeyful (Oveporexton)
