As illustrated in Table 79, the most common TEAEs leading to discontinuation in the LTE
study, ranked by number of events, were:
Liver enzyme elevations (n=6): Six participants met protocol defined discontinuation criteria
due to liver enzyme elevations, including one participant with concurrent infectious
mononucleosis.
Most cases had alternative etiologies that could explain the LFT abnormalities, including
infectious mononucleosis, intense physical exercise with concurrent rhabdomyolysis,
underlying hepatic steatosis, and weight gain. For a detailed review of hepatotoxicity
assessment and causality determination, the reader should refer to the clinical review
conducted by the DILI team.
Insomnia (n=4): Five participants discontinued due to insomnia events, including one
participant with concurrent pollakiuria; all of which were mild-to-moderate in severity and
considered related to the trial intervention.
These events represent an expected on-target pharmacological effect of oveporexton and are
consistent with the known safety profile established in the pivotal trials.
219
Reference ID: 5847402

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 220860
Orzeyful (Oveporexton)
Breast cancer (n=2): Two participants discontinued due to breast cancer. The two breast
cancer cases occurred in women aged 42 and 48 years during regular follow-up. The events
occurred 523 and 608 days after treatment initiation, respectively. We discussed the two
breast cancer cases with the nonclinical team, the Division of Epidemiology, and the Division
of Oncology. Given that these cases occurred at 523 and 608 days after treatment initiation in
relatively young women (aged 42 and 48 years), we considered whether the timing could be
consistent with a causal relationship with the drug. The nonclinical team confirmed that there
was no drug-related increase in mammary gland tumors in the 2-year carcinogenicity study in
rats. Considering the high background prevalence of breast cancer in the general population
and the absence of nonclinical signals, these cases are likely attributable to background
incidence and are not considered related to oveporexton treatment. The Division of
Epidemiology and the Division of Oncology agreed with this conclusion. (See Section 8.2.8.1
Subsection Human Carcinogenicity or Tumor Development for more details.)
Other individual events (n=1 each): The remaining TEAEs leading to trial intervention
discontinuation included blood pressure increased, dysgeusia, myocardial infarction,
pollakiuria, and weight increased, each occurring in one participant.
The single case of blood pressure increased leading to discontinuation occurred in a
participant with inconsistent compliance, raising concern that the participant may have been
taking higher dosages than recommended. This confounding factor limits the interpretability
of this event as a treatment-related safety signal.

Other Trials
In Study TAK-861-1001, there were two cases of insomnia, one case of SARS-CoV-2 test
positive, and one case of blood pressure diastolic increased leading to discontinuation.
The insomnia cases are likely related to oveporexton and represent an expected on-target
pharmacological effect consistent with the drug's profile. The case of SARS-CoV-2 test positive
220
Reference ID: 5847402

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 220860
Orzeyful (Oveporexton)
is not related to trial intervention. The case of diastolic blood pressure increase occurred in a
participant receiving a supratherapeutic dose of 30 mg oveporexton (15 times the maximum
recommended dose), and a causal relationship with oveporexton cannot be excluded.
90-Day Safety Updates
Since the original data cut-off date, two participants discontinued the study due to TEAEs:
•

A participant with an SAE of acute myeloid leukemia that was diagnosed on Day 245

•

A participant with a severe TEAE of cataplexy that began on Day 117

The acute myeloid leukemia case has been discussed previously under the SAE–90 days
updates subsection. For the second case of cataplexy, cataplexy is a sign and symptom of the
underlying disorder and is unlikely related to the treatment.
In summary, no new safety concerns emerged with extended treatment duration. The
majority of discontinuations do not appear to be related to trial intervention, and the pattern
of events is consistent with the known safety profile established in the pivotal trials. Routine
postmarketing surveillance and regular postmarketing safety updates from the Applicant will
allow FDA to continue to monitor the safety of oveporexton in the postmarket setting.
Significant Adverse Events
Pivotal Trial Pool
A total of eight participants experienced a severe TEAE during the pivotal trial pool, with
equal distribution between the oveporexton and placebo groups (6/196 [3.1%] versus 2/76
[2.6%], respectively).
In the oveporexton group, three participants experienced severe insomnia. These events
occurred on Day 1 and resolved within 3 to 4 days without requiring changes to study
medication (two participants on oveporexton 2 mg BID, one on 1 mg BID). Two participants
on oveporexton 2 mg BID experienced severe pollakiuria beginning on Day 1 or 2, which
resolved after 2 days in one case and after 21 days in the second case, both without
treatment modifications.
There was one case of severe ureterolithiasis in the oveporexton group that required
treatment discontinuation and has been described in the treatment discontinuation
paragraph. One of the participants who experienced insomnia also experienced severe
erythema on Day 63, which resolved after 12 days.
In the placebo group, one participant experienced a severe molar infection on Day 67 and
continued treatment. Another participant experienced severe increases of AST/ALT that
required treatment discontinuation, as per protocol, and has been described in the treatment
discontinuation subsection.
The incidence of severe TEAEs was low and comparable between oveporexton and placebo
groups. The majority of severe events in the oveporexton group were insomnia and
221
Reference ID: 5847402

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 220860
Orzeyful (Oveporexton)
pollakiuria, which are consistent with the known pharmacologic effects of orexin receptor
agonism. These events were generally self-limiting and did not require treatment
discontinuation.
Other Integrated Summary of Safety Studies
Study TAK-861-2003
In the LTE study, excluding the fatal TEAE of cardiac arrest previously described, 16
participants (4.5% of 356 total participants) experienced 17 severe TEAEs.
One severe TEAE (blood pressure increase) required treatment discontinuation and has been
previously described. Three severe TEAEs—one case of optic neuritis, one case of Achilles
tendon rupture, and one case of myocardial infarction—were also coded as SAEs and have
been previously described. See the appropriate subsections in this review for these cases.
The remaining 13 severe TEAEs are described below.
The majority of severe TEAEs were related to urinary symptoms and sleep disturbances,
which are recognized on-target pharmacological effects of orexin receptor agonism. Six cases
of severe insomnia were reported across treatment groups: three cases occurred in
participants receiving 1 mg BID, two cases in the 2 mg BID group, and one case in the 7 mg QD
group. Additionally, one case of severe pollakiuria (increased urinary frequency) and one case
of severe micturition urgency were reported in participants receiving 2 mg BID.
Two severe events related to musculoskeletal injuries were reported in participants receiving
2 mg BID: one severe limb injury (puncture wound to the hand) and one severe foot fracture.
These events were assessed as unrelated to trial intervention.
Three additional severe events were reported, each occurring in a single participant: severe
COVID-19, severe diarrhea, and severe tooth pain. These events were assessed as unrelated
to trial intervention.
The severe TEAEs observed in this long-term extension study are consistent with the known
safety profile of orexin receptor agonists as a class. The predominance of insomnia and
urinary events (pollakiuria and micturition urgency) among the severe TEAEs reflects the
expected on-target pharmacological effects of OX2R agonism. These events are wellcharacterized for this drug class and do not represent unexpected safety signals. The
musculoskeletal injuries and other likely unrelated events (COVID-19, diarrhea, tooth pain)
are consistent with background rates expected in this patient population during long-term
follow-up and do not raise concerns regarding the safety profile of oveporexton.
Study TAK-861-2001
Seven participants in Study TAK-861-2001 experienced severe AEs during the trial. Some
participants experienced more than one severe AEs:
•

The most common severe AE was insomnia, which affected four participants. Three cases
occurred on Day 1, and one case occurred on Day 2. None of these participants required
222

Reference ID: 5847402

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 220860
Orzeyful (Oveporexton)
specific treatment for insomnia, and all continued taking the study medication without any
dosage changes.
•

Two participants experienced severe cataplexy episodes. Neither participant stopped taking
the medication.

•

Two participants experienced severe headaches. Both cases occurred on Day 2 of
treatment and were considered related to oveporexton. One headache resolved the same
day it started. The other resolved within 2 days, though a milder headache came back
briefly on Day 3 and was treated with a single dose of paracetamol (acetaminophen).

•

One participant experienced severe mood swings and vomiting on Day 2. Both events
resolved within 2 days.

•

One participant experienced severe COVID-19-related adverse events (cough, fever, muscle
pain, and nasal congestion) on Day 83, which occurred during the follow-up period after
completing the study medication.

•

One participant experienced a severe ankle fracture. This case was coded as serious and
has been described in the section related to serious adverse events (SAEs).

Severe AEs observed in Study TAK-861-2001 were generally consistent with the known safety
profile of oveporexton. The most frequently reported severe adverse event was insomnia,
which is an expected effect of this medication given its mechanism of action as a wakepromoting agent for the treatment of NT1. The severe cataplexy episodes reported in two
participants are of particular interest, as cataplexy is a core symptom of NT1 that
oveporexton is intended to treat. One case involved worsening of cataplexy that persisted
throughout the trial, while the other was a transient episode that resolved the same day.
These events may reflect either inadequate symptom control or natural fluctuation in disease
severity rather than a drug-induced effect.
The severe headaches, mood swings, vomiting, and COVID-19 infection were isolated events
that either resolved quickly or were likely unrelated to study medication (in the case of
COVID-19). The ankle fracture, described separately as a serious adverse event, represents a
traumatic injury and it is unlikely related to drug exposure.
90-Day Safety Update
The overall severity of TEAEs in the 90-day safety update was consistent with that reported in
the original submission. The only change was in the overall total number of severe adverse
events, which decreased by one from the original submission (the original total included one
severe TEAE that was subsequently downgraded to moderate).
In summary, the incidence of severe TEAEs across the clinical development program was low
and consistent with the known pharmacologic profile of orexin receptor agonism. The
majority of severe events in oveporexton-treated participants were insomnia and urinary
symptoms (pollakiuria and micturition urgency), which are expected on-target effects of OX2R
agonism. These events were generally self-limiting, occurred early in treatment, and rarely
required treatment discontinuation.

of TEAEs in each oveporexton dosage group compared to placebo.

The most common TEAEs in the oveporexton group at a rate greater than placebo were
insomnia (56% versus 1%), pollakiuria (57% versus 5%), micturition urgency (16% versus 1%),
and salivary hypersecretion (7% versus 0%).
These results were confirmed when events were clustered by system organ class or OND
broad and narrow queries (data not shown).
The nearly two-fold increase in overall TEAE incidence with oveporexton compared to
placebo (87% versus 49%) is driven primarily by expected on-target pharmacologic effects of
orexin receptor agonism. The most common TEAEs—insomnia (56% versus 1%), pollakiuria
(57% versus 5%), micturition urgency (16% versus 1%), and salivary hypersecretion (7% versus
227
Reference ID: 5847402

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 220860
Orzeyful (Oveporexton)
0%)—are consistent with the known effects of increased wakefulness and orexinergic
signaling on sleep architecture, bladder function, and autonomic regulation.
The high incidence of insomnia is notable but expected given the wake-promoting mechanism
of action. This finding underscores the importance of appropriate dose timing and patient
counseling regarding sleep hygiene. Similarly, the urinary symptoms (pollakiuria and
micturition urgency) reflect orexin's known role in bladder control and are consistent with the
class effects observed with other orexin-acting drugs. Salivary hypersecretion has also been
reported with other orexin receptor agonists, consistent with a class effect of orexinergic
stimulation.
The remaining TEAEs occurred at low frequencies and were generally balanced between
treatment groups or consistent with background rates in this population. Importantly,
hypersomnia occurred less frequently in the oveporexton group compared to placebo, which
may reflect therapeutic benefit.
Of note, the generally low incidence of CPK increase as a TEAE, even lower in the oveporexton
group compared to the placebo group, does not align with the incidence of blood level
measurements of CPK observed to be >5× ULN (see below data on chemistry results). See the
discussion following Table 86.
The TEAE profile is acceptable and manageable for the treatment of NT1, provided that
appropriate labeling addresses the common on-target effects.

232
Reference ID: 5847402

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 220860
Orzeyful (Oveporexton)
Pollakiuria, insomnia, and micturition urgency continue to represent the most common
TEAEs, although with a lower frequency than the one reported in the pivotal trials. All of
these events appear to be dose-dependent and are consistent with the known
pharmacological effect of oveporexton on orexin receptor-2.
Nasopharyngitis, headache, and influenza were also among the most frequently reported
TEAEs (>10%).
The LTE lacks a placebo comparator; however, TEAEs may inform the safety profile associated
with long-term administration. The types of common TEAEs observed during long-term
treatment are consistent with those seen in the short-term studies (i.e., pollakiuria, insomnia,
and micturition urgency), although occurring at lower frequencies. This may suggest a
decrease in TEAE incidence over time with continued exposure to oveporexton, given that
most participants rolled over from the controlled studies to the LTE.
Nasopharyngitis, headache, and influenza were common as well across exposure intervals,
with incidence comparable to background rates seen in real-world populations
(https://www.who.int/, https://www.informedhealth.org/) and unlikely related to trial
intervention.
Of note, musculoskeletal injuries were reported during the LTE study, including fractures
(total n=3, 0.8%; foot fracture, hand fracture, and tibia fracture) and other injuries (total
n=11, 3.1%; limb injury in four participants, joint injury in two participants, meniscus injury in
two participants, and severe Achilles tendon rupture in one participant; the latter has been
described in detail in the SAE section). Additionally, seven participants (2.0%) experienced
falls during the study.
Given the relatively high number of fractures and joint injuries observed in the LTE, as well as
the occurrence of falls, we conducted a retrospective analysis of these events in the pivotal
trial pool. This analysis revealed no difference in the incidence of musculoskeletal injuries or
falls between the placebo and oveporexton groups; the incidence was slightly higher in the
placebo group (n=2, 1.0% in the oveporexton overall group versus n=2, 2.6% in the placebo
group, both joint injury and contusion, no cases of fracture or fall in the pivotal trial pool). In
addition, review of the available narratives did not suggest a consistent pattern or mechanism
linking these events to oveporexton exposure. Based on these findings, we have no safety
concern at this time and conclude that the observed events represent background incidences.
Per the Applicant's report, the falls were not related to NT1 symptoms. Routine postmarketing surveillance is sufficient to collect additional data on this potential safety signal,
although the current data do not suggest a rate different from background incidence.
90-Day Safety Update
The overall severity of TEAEs in the 90-day safety update was consistent with that reported in
the original submission. The only changes were in the overall total number of back pain,
blood creatine phosphokinase increased, and cataplexy, which increased by 2, 3, and 1 event,
respectively, resulting in the proportion of these events exceeding 5%.

233
Reference ID: 5847402

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 220860
Orzeyful (Oveporexton)
The event of cataplexy was severe and required trial discontinuation. This case has been
described in the section addressing discontinuations in the 90-day safety update.
In summary, the TEAE profile of oveporexton is characterized predominantly by expected ontarget pharmacologic effects related to orexin receptor agonism, including insomnia,
pollakiuria, micturition urgency, and salivary hypersecretion. These events occurred at higher
incidences in the oveporexton group compared to placebo in the pivotal trials but decreased
in frequency during long-term exposure in the LTE study, suggesting potential tolerance or
adaptation over time. The musculoskeletal injuries and falls observed in the LTE do not
appear to be treatment-related based on retrospective analysis of the pivotal trial pool,
where rates were comparable to or lower than placebo. The overall safety profile is
acceptable and manageable for the treatment of NT1, provided that appropriate labeling
addresses the common on-target effects and patients receive counseling regarding dosing
timing and sleep hygiene.
Laboratory Findings
Overall, laboratory results in the pivotal trial pool and in the LTE study did not show any
majorly concerning clinical or toxicity-related issues, and no cases meeting Hy’s law. See the
DILI team consult for a detailed assessment of potential DILI signals and liver enzyme
elevations.
Pivotal Trial Pool
Hematology
As described in the Schedule of Assessments of Studies TAK-861-3001 and TAK-861-3002,
hematology laboratory tests were assessed at Screening, Baseline (Day -2), Week 4 (Day 28),
Week 8 (Day 56), Week 12, (Day 84), and at Follow-up 7 days after last dose.
The clinical reviewer’s examination of hematological parameter measures of central tendency
(e.g., mean, median) found no clinically significant changes of note over the treatment period
and results were generally balanced across arms (Table 82).

The clinical reviewer’s examination of shifts from normal baseline to abnormal results at any
timepoint found generally low numbers of such shifts across parameters, and generally
balanced numbers across arms (Table 83). A small numerical difference in the proportion of
participants with Level 1 low hemoglobin was observed in the oveporexton 2 mg BID arm
(5.1%) compared to placebo (0%); however, no participants met the Level 2 or Level 3
thresholds in any active treatment arm, and no dose-dependent pattern was observed.
Similarly, a small difference in Level 1 low lymphocytes was noted, although the placebo arm
showed a higher proportion of participants with low lymphocytes than either active
treatment arm (6.6% versus 3.7% and 0%, respectively). Neither finding is considered to
reflect a drug-related effect, and both are likely attributable to chance and of no clinical
significance.

Orzeyful (Oveporexton)
The clinical reviewer’s examination of shifts from normal baseline to abnormal results at any
time points found generally low numbers of such shifts across parameters, generally balanced
across arms, except for bicarbonate, with higher percentage of lower Level 2 (<18 mEq/L) in
the oveporexton groups compared to placebo (16/196, 8.2% versus 1/76, 1.3% respectively).
The clinical meaning of this change is likely negligible, given that there were not TEAEs related
to this chemistry change reported in the adae.xpt dataset, such as fatigue, nausea, and
hyperventilation.
Although not statistically significant, CPK values were higher in the oveporexton groups
compared to placebo at all levels. For example, Level 2 (markedly abnormal value (MAV); >5×
ULN) occurred in 21 participants (10%) in the oveporexton groups and 4 participants (5%) in
the placebo group (Table 86). However, of the 21 cases of increased CPK observed in the
oveporexton group, only 19% (n=4/21) were reported as TEAEs in the adae.xpt dataset,
whereas of the four cases of increased CPK in the placebo group, the majority, 75% (n=3/4),
were reported as TEAEs in the adae.xpt dataset (Table 80).

An IR was sent to the Applicant to clarify the specific criteria used to determine whether an
elevated CK/CPK value was coded as a TEAE, and to explain why some cases were coded as
TEAEs while others were not. The Applicant responded that “no specific criteria or numeric
thresholds were used to determine whether an elevated CK value was coded as a TEAE.
Instead, per the protocol, abnormal laboratory tests were considered AEs when judged
240
Reference ID: 5847402

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 220860
Orzeyful (Oveporexton)
clinically significant by the investigator." Thus, variability in whether elevated CK values were
coded as TEAEs reflects differences in investigator reporting rather than the application of
specific laboratory thresholds or clinical criteria. Therefore, the comparison of TEAEs for
increased CPK between treatment groups is not reliable. We requested narratives for each
participant with increased blood CPK to review.
The comprehensive review of CK elevation cases (narratives) from both TAK-861-3001 and
TAK-861-3002 studies reveals a pattern of transient, asymptomatic CK elevations that resolve
spontaneously without intervention and are consistently attributed to alternative causes such
as physical activity. Among all participants who experienced CK elevations above the upper
limit of normal, 22 were receiving oveporexton and six were receiving placebo. Of the 22
cases in the oveporexton group, 14 (63.6%) were attributed to physical activity, with the
remaining eight cases explained by other identifiable causes such as intercurrent illness (e.g.,
influenza; 2 cases) or unknown etiology. Of the six cases in the placebo group, three (50%)
were associated with physical activity, with the remaining three attributable to other causes.
CK elevations >5×ULN occurred in 21/196 (10.7%) participants in the oveporexton group
compared to 4/76 (5.3%) participants in the placebo group. Some cases reached markedly
elevated levels (>16,500 U/L; 10,402 U/L; 6,297 U/L). All cases were asymptomatic, none
demonstrated myoglobinuria or renal impairment, and all resolved without specific
intervention. In two cases, the drug was discontinued due to concomitant increases in liver
enzymes per protocol-defined discontinuation criteria (refer to subsection related to
discontinuation in the pivotal trials pool).
CPK values and the differences observed across treatment groups will be described in the
Warnings and Precautions section of the product labeling.
Lipids
There was a higher percentage of participants with cholesterol and LDL levels exceeding level
2 and level 3 at any timepoint post-baseline in the oveporexton groups compared to placebo
(43% versus 28% respectively for Level 3). However, a modest, consistent difference in LDL
cholesterol was observed between the oveporexton and placebo groups in the 12-week
pivotal trials (Figure 24). The mean change from Baseline at Week 12 was approximately
+14 mg/dL higher in the oveporexton groups compared to placebo. This difference was
consistent across both pivotal studies (TAK-861-3001 and TAK-861-3002) and across multiple
timepoints; while the magnitude of change is small and unlikely to be markedly clinically
meaningful, it was a consistent trend.
Total cholesterol showed a similar pattern, with a mean change of approximately +12 mg/dL in
oveporexton groups versus essentially no change in placebo at Week 12. HDL cholesterol
showed minimal changes in both treatment groups (approximately -3.7 mg/dL in the
oveporexton groups compared to -1.7 mg/dL in placebo), and triglycerides actually decreased
slightly in both groups (approximately -314.6 mg/dL in the oveporexton groups compared to 11.7 mg/dL in placebo) (Table 87 and Figure 24).

Figure 24. Mean Lipids Data Change From Baseline Over Time by Treatment Arm, Safety
Population, Trials TAK-861-3001 and TAK-861-3002

Abbreviations: CI, confidence interval; HDL, high-density lipoprotein; LDL, low-density lipoprotein
Duration is 12 weeks.
The vertical bars shown on the plotted lines indicate the 95% confidence interval of the mean change at the corresponding time points.
Participants with both baseline and postbaseline data available are included in the mean change from baseline calculations at each visit. The
number of participants reflects only those included in the mean change from baseline calculations, rather than the total number of participants.
Figures do not include time points with data from fewer than 10% of randomized/enrolled participants in all treatment groups as the results
would not be considered a reliable indicator of the true mean.
Only central laboratory data are included in the analysis. equation.
Source: Clinical Data Scientist’s team; adlb.xpt; Software: R

Although a higher proportion of participants in the oveporexton arms met threshold criteria
for elevated total cholesterol and LDL levels compared to placebo, and both oveporexton
dosage groups showed modest, broadly comparable decreases in HDL cholesterol from
baseline without a discernible dose-response relationship, this review team does not consider
these findings to represent a clinically meaningful safety concern at this time. It is worth
noting that total cholesterol is mathematically derived from its components, including LDL,
and therefore the two findings are not independent. Additionally, no dose-dependent effect
was observed for LDL cholesterol, as elevations were broadly similar across both dosage
groups, further limiting the inferential weight of this finding. More importantly, the broader
metabolic picture does not support a pattern consistent with a drug-induced metabolic
syndrome or increased cardiovascular risk: triglycerides were similar to placebo, and there
were no TEAEs related to metabolic syndrome or cardiovascular risk observed at a higher rate
in the oveporexton arms relative to placebo.
In the absence of a concerning metabolic profile and given the small magnitude of the LDL
changes, the modestly higher proportion of participants with elevated LDL in the oveporexton
groups is not considered clinically significant to the extent that a labeling warning is required.
246
Reference ID: 5847402

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 220860
Orzeyful (Oveporexton)
Interpretation of these findings is further limited by the small sample sizes across treatment
arms, which reduce the reliability of between-group comparisons and preclude definitive
conclusions. However, due to the consistent trends in the studies (including the LTE study to
be discussed below), the laboratory data will be described in Section 6 of labeling.
Other Integrated Summary of Safety Studies
LTE Trial (TAK-861-2003)
As observed in the pivotal trial pool, the increase in CPK that met MAV criteria (>5× ULN)
continued in the LTE (n=36, 10%). Of these cases, n=12 were reported as TEAEs of blood CPK
increased in the TEAE adae.xpt dataset. However, all cases were asymptomatic, and the
Applicant reported no cases of rhabdomyolysis. These cases were confounded by other
potential etiologies, including exercise (n=29) and viral infections (n=3).
No other abnormal laboratory values were reported.
Notwithstanding the inherent limitations of direct comparisons between label extension
studies and placebo-controlled pivotal trials, including high dropout rates, differences in
patient populations, variable exposure durations, and other confounding factors, an IR was
sent to the Applicant to further characterize the trend of LDL increases observed in the LTE
study, with the specific aim of excluding any major or unusual shifts. As illustrated in Table 88,
no such shifts were identified; the proportions of participants with LDL values exceeding
specified thresholds in the LTE study were consistent with those observed in the Pivotal Trial
Pool, providing some additional reassurance regarding the long-term lipid profile of
oveporexton.

Mean lipid values in the LTE study are presented in Table 89, Table 90, Table 91, and Table 92.
At the LTE baseline, mean total cholesterol, LDL cholesterol, HDL cholesterol, and triglycerides
were within or near normal limits and were broadly consistent with values observed at the
end of the parent studies.
Small, stable increases in mean total cholesterol and LDL cholesterol from LTE baseline were
observed across all timepoints and dosage groups. Mean HDL cholesterol showed small
numerical decreases from baseline across all timepoints, without evidence of a progressive or
dose-dependent trend. Mean triglycerides were similarly stable, with small numerical
decreases from baseline at most timepoints; a small numerical increase observed at Week
104 is likely attributable to the substantially reduced sample size at that visit rather than a
treatment effect.

Overall, the LTE lipid data do not suggest a clinically meaningful effect of long-term
oveporexton treatment on lipid metabolism but still show a slight trend towards mild
elevation that plateaus. Direct comparison across timepoints and dosage groups is limited by
the absence of a concurrent placebo arm, time-varying exposure due to dosage adjustments,
and declining sample sizes at later visits. Nevertheless, product labeling should reflect the
observed LDL increases.

252
Reference ID: 5847402

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 220860
Orzeyful (Oveporexton)
90-Day Safety Update
An additional eight participants experienced CPK elevation that met MAV criteria (>5× ULN).
Two of these cases were reported as TEAEs:
•

One participant experienced a TEAE of PT “rhabdomyolysis” (participant also had an
increase in AST), which was mild and resolved while on treatment.

•

One participant experienced a TEAE of blood CPK increase ("investigations"), severe, that
resolved within 3 days. No clinical symptoms were associated.

No other abnormal laboratory values were reported.
The presence of confounding factors (exercise, viral infections) in the majority of cases of CPK
elevations in the LTE study and its 90-day safety update, combined with the asymptomatic
nature of most elevations and spontaneous resolution, suggests these events are likely
multifactorial rather than directly drug-related. However, a trend of higher CPK elevations
was also observed in the pivotal trial pool, with higher levels in the oveporexton group
compared to placebo. Although there was not a higher incidence of TEAEs in the oveporexton
group compared to placebo, there were two cases of PT “rhabdomyolysis” (but without dark
urine, myalgia, or renal impairment) leading to discontinuation in the oveporexton group
compared to zero cases in the placebo group, plus an additional case in the LTE 90-day safety
update. Although there were confounding factors (excessive exercise in one case, routine
exercise associated with Gilbert's syndrome in another case, and no apparent cofactor in the
third mild case), we cannot exclude a causal relationship with the study drug.
In summary, while the majority of evidence suggests CPK elevations are likely multifactorial
and primarily related to alternative causes (particularly physical activity), a causal relationship
with oveporexton cannot be definitively excluded. The higher incidence in oveporextontreated participants compared to placebo, the persistence of this pattern across the pivotal
trials and LTE study, and the occurrence of three cases labeled as "rhabdomyolysis" (albeit
with confounding factors and not meeting clinical criteria) warrant inclusion of
comprehensive information about CPK elevations in product labeling.
An important consideration in causality assessment is the therapeutic context. Patients with
narcolepsy often experience significant functional impairment and reduced physical activity
due to excessive daytime sleepiness. Effective treatment may lead to increased energy levels
and return to physical activities, which could independently contribute to CPK elevations. This
creates a challenge in distinguishing between a direct drug effect and an indirect effect
mediated through improved function and increased activity.
The absence of true rhabdomyolysis (defined by the triad of muscle symptoms,
myoglobinuria, and renal impairment), the uniformly benign clinical course, and the
consistent presence of confounding factors provide reassurance that oveporexton does not
appear to cause clinically significant muscle toxicity. However, transparency regarding the
observed laboratory findings is essential for informed prescribing and appropriate patient
monitoring, particularly in patients who may be at higher risk (e.g., those taking concomitant

253
Reference ID: 5847402

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 220860
Orzeyful (Oveporexton)
statins or other myotoxic medications, those with underlying muscle disorders, or those
engaging in strenuous physical activity).
The modest increases in LDL and total cholesterol, while consistently trending higher on drug
versus placebo and also in the LTE study (and corresponding mild decrease in HDL), are of
minimal clinical significance given their small magnitude and the absence of any
cardiovascular or lipid-related adverse events. Standard lipid monitoring as part of routine
clinical care would be appropriate, but these findings do not warrant specific warnings or
precautions in labeling beyond inclusion of the lab value trends in the adverse reactions
section with appropriate context.
Vital Signs
Pivotal Trial Pool
As described, vital signs including blood pressure, heart rate, body weight, respiratory rate,
and temperature were measured at Screening, Baseline, Week 4 (Day 28), Week 8 (Day 56),
Week 12 (Day 84), and 7 days after last dose (Day 91). Per protocol, height was measured at
Screening and Week 12 only.
Blood Pressure and Heart Rate
First, we examined the adae.xpt dataset to identify any TEAEs related to the cardiovascular
system organ class. There was one case of clinically significant blood pressure increase and
one case of palpitations in the oveporexton group (one in the 1 mg BID group and one in the
2 mg BID group), compared to no cases in the placebo group. There was one case of
hypotension in the placebo group.
In addition, two cases in the oveporexton group reported chest pain, one of which was
serious and has been described previously. Comprehensive cardiac workup of the SAE
revealed no coronary or structural abnormalities.
The clinical reviewer's examination of vital signs parameter measures of SBP, DBP, and HR
found no changes of note over time, with a mild increase in mean BP and HR observed on Day
1 in the overall oveporexton group, as illustrated in Figure 25, Figure 26, Figure 27 and Table
96. However, the changes were minimal (SBP mean increase of 7 mm Hg, DBP mean increase
of 5 mm Hg, and HR mean increase of 5 bpm) and returned to near baseline values by Week
12 (see also Table 93, Table 94, Table 95, and Table 96).

Figures do not include time points with data from fewer than 10% of randomized/enrolled subjects in all treatment groups as the results would
not be considered a reliable indicator of the true mean.

Body Weight—Respiratory Rate—Temperature
Based on the TEAE adae.xpt dataset, a total of 4.5% (9/196) of participants experienced
weight increase in the oveporexton group compared to 3.9% (3/76) in the placebo group. The
clinical reviewer's examination of weight changes from baseline during the 12-week
treatment found a higher percentage of participants with ≥5% weight gain in the placebo
group compared to the oveporexton groups (28% [21/76] versus 20% [39/196], respectively).
Mean body weight increased modestly from baseline across all treatment groups over the 12week double-blind period. At Week 12, patients receiving oveporexton 1 mg (n=57) gained a
mean of 1.6 kg from baseline, while those receiving oveporexton 2 mg (n=133) gained 0.9 kg;
the combined oveporexton 1 to 2 mg group (n=190) gained a mean of 1.1 kg. In comparison,
placebo-treated patients (n=70) gained a mean of 2.3 kg over the same period. The difference
in mean change from baseline between the combined oveporexton group and placebo was
−1.2 kg, indicating that patients on oveporexton gained approximately 1.2 kg less than those
on placebo over 12 weeks.
There were no TEAEs related to increased or decreased respiratory rate and temperature in
the adae.xpt dataset. The clinical reviewer's examination of respiratory rate and temperature
changes from baseline during the 12-week treatment did not find a notable difference
between groups as measured by central tendency (mean, median).
In summary, the evaluation of vital signs in the pivotal trial pool revealed no major clinically
significant safety concerns with oveporexton treatment. Cardiovascular assessments showed
minimal and transient increases in blood pressure and heart rate on Day 1 (mean increases of
7 mm Hg SBP, 5 mm Hg DBP, and 5 bpm HR), which returned to near baseline values by Week
12. The incidence of post-baseline outlier values for blood pressure and heart rate was similar
between oveporexton and placebo groups. While two cases of chest pain were reported in
the oveporexton group (including one serious adverse event), comprehensive cardiac workup
revealed no coronary or structural abnormalities.
Body weight changes were comparable between groups, with a slightly lower percentage of
participants experiencing ≥5% weight gain in the oveporexton groups compared to placebo
260
Reference ID: 5847402

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 220860
Orzeyful (Oveporexton)
(19.9% versus 27.6%, respectively). Mean weight change from baseline was also numerically
smaller in the combined oveporexton group compared to placebo at Week 12. Overall, these
findings do not suggest any clear effect of oveporexton on body weight.
Respiratory rate and temperature measurements showed no notable differences between
treatment groups. Overall, no clinically significant adverse trends were observed in the
evaluation of vital signs parameters during the 12-week treatment period.
Other Integrated Summary of Safety Studies
LTE Trial (TAK-861-2003)
Blood Pressure and Heart Rate
First, we examined the adae.xpt dataset to identify any TEAEs related to the cardiovascular
system organ class. There were seven participants (2.0%) that experienced a TEAEs related to
BP or HR (three palpitations, two BP increased, one diastolic BP increased, one HR increased,
and one hypertension), all mild to moderate, except for the participant who experienced a
severe BP increase that resulted in trial intervention discontinuation and already described in
the paragraph related to treatment discontinuation in the LTE TAK-861-2003 Study (the
participant had inconsistent compliance, raising concern that the participant may have been
taking higher dosages than recommended).
This clinical reviewer's examination of vital signs parameter measures of SBP, DBP, and HR
found the mean change from Baseline to Month 6 of treatment for BP (both SBP and DBP)
was <5 mm Hg, and HR was not changed meaningfully compared with baseline values at all
time points.
The MAV analysis revealed that SBP values ≥160 mm Hg were observed in two participants
(0.6%), and DBP values ≥100 mm Hg were observed in six participants (1.7%). All participants
with SBP values >160 mm Hg had isolated single visit elevations. Only one participant had
DBP >100 mm Hg at three visits; however, their SBP values remained within normal limits and
no TEAEs of BP increased were reported.
Body Weight—Respiratory Rate—Temperature
Based on the TEAE adae.xpt dataset, a total of 3.7% (13/356) of participants experienced
weight increase in the oveporexton group in the LTE study. Body weight increase AEs did not
appear to be dose-related (1-mg BID group = 0, 2-mg BID group = 3.3%, 2-mg/5-mg group =
2.6%, 7-mg group = 5%). There was one case of weight decrease in the LTE study as well.
Mean changes in weight in the LTE study are presented in Figure 28.

261
Reference ID: 5847402

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 220860
Orzeyful (Oveporexton)
Figure 28. Mean Change From Baseline in Weight (kg) (Long-Term Extension Study TAK-8612003, NT1 Safety Set, Data Cutoff: August 29, 2025)

Source: Clinical reviewer created, from data in Applicant’s CSR Study TAK-861-2003, Table 15.3.5.3.1

The LTE baseline is defined as the last non-missing weight value prior to the first LTE dose
(overall mean: 80.9 kg). Briefly, small and stable increases in mean weight from LTE baseline
were observed across all dosage groups and timepoints, ranging from approximately +1.6 to
+1.8 kg through Week 52, with a modest numerical increase at Weeks 78 and 104 that is likely
attributable to the substantially smaller sample sizes at those visits rather than a progressive
drug effect. Across all timepoints and dosage groups, weight changes were small in absolute
terms and did not suggest a clinically meaningful effect of long-term oveporexton treatment
on body weight. The magnitude of weight change observed in the LTE is broadly consistent
with that seen in the oveporexton-treated arms of the pivotal studies (placebo-treated
patients gained a mean of 2.3 kg over the 12-week period in the pivotal studies), though
direct comparison and interpretation is limited by the absence of a placebo arm in the LTE
and the declining sample sizes at later visits.
MAVs for respiratory rate and temperature vital signs were consistent with those in the
pivotal trials pool.
Although the absence of a placebo-controlled arm in the LTE study limits the ability to
definitively attribute these vital sign findings to background rates versus drug effect, the
overall data are consistent with the pivotal trial placebo-controlled studies and do not reveal
any new or unexpected safety signals. The low incidence of cardiovascular-related adverse
262
Reference ID: 5847402

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 220860
Orzeyful (Oveporexton)
events, minimal mean changes in blood pressure and heart rate, infrequent occurrence of
markedly abnormal blood pressure values, and absence of dose-related patterns for weight
increase over several months provide reassurance regarding the cardiovascular and general
vital sign safety profile of oveporexton during long-term treatment.
90-Day Safety Update
Blood Pressure and Heart Rate
Since the original submission, TEAEs of palpitations, hypertension, and metabolic syndrome
were reported in one participant each. These TEAEs were mild and did not require dosage
changes.
Body Weight—Respiratory Rate—Temperature
No changes were observed in the 90-day safety update related to body weight, respiratory
rate, or temperature.
Electrocardiograms
Pivotal Trial Pool
As described, standard 12-lead ECGs were collected and reviewed locally (per the protocol),
including heart rate, sinus rhythm PR, QRS, and QTcF, with QTc analysis. QTcF values were
flagged for a post-dose QTcF >450 msec in male or >470 msec in female, ΔQTcF ≥60 msec,
and QT ≥500 msec. ECG was measured at Day 1, Week 4 (Day 28), Week 8 (Day 55) and Week
12 (Day 83) in both studies TAK861-3001 and TAK-861-3002.
As shown in Table 97, at Week 12, ECG parameters were generally similar across treatment
groups. Mean changes from baseline in ECG heart rate, PR interval, QRS duration, and QTcF
interval were small and comparable between the oveporexton 1-mg BID, 2-mg BID, and
placebo groups.

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 220860
Orzeyful (Oveporexton)
We then explored MAVs in ECG results at all postdose visits:
•

PR interval: More subjects in the oveporexton group (4.6%, 9/195) had PR interval
≥200 msec compared to 1.3% (1/76) in the placebo group, and this did not appear to be
dose-related (there was a higher percentage in the 1 mg BID group compared to the 2-mg
BID group, 6.8% versus 3.7%, respectively). Per the Applicant's report, none were assessed
as clinically significant.

•

QRS duration: A slightly higher percentage of participants in the oveporexton group, 16.9%
(33/195, equally distributed between 1-mg BID and 2-mg BID subgroups), had a QRS interval
≤80 msec versus 13.2% (10/76) in the placebo group. None were assessed as clinically
significant.

•

QTcF interval: One subject in the oveporexton group (in the 2-mg BID subgroup) had a QTcF
interval change from baseline ≥60 msec, compared to no participants in the placebo group.
This was not assessed as clinically significant.

Regarding ECG interpretation, the percentage of participants with "abnormal" ECG readings
was similar across groups (Baseline: 6.6% placebo versus 10.2% oveporexton; Week 4: 11.1%
placebo versus 10.4% oveporexton) or slightly higher in the placebo group compared to the
oveporexton group (Week 8: 10% placebo versus 6.3% oveporexton; Week 12: 13% placebo
versus 4.8% oveporexton).
However, there was one participant (Participant ID
) receiving oveporexton 1 mg
BID whose abnormal ECG was considered clinically significant: this participant had clinically
significantly abnormal ECG results at Baseline and through Week 8 of the trial which were not
considered a TEAE. Given that the participant had a clinically significant abnormal ECG at
Baseline, it is unlikely this was related to study drug. An IR was sent to the Applicant to clarify
why this participant was enrolled in the study despite having a clinically significant abnormal
ECG at Baseline. The Applicant responded clarifying that the participant was appropriately
enrolled, as the observed finding—a T wave inversion at the baseline visit—did not meet any
protocol-defined exclusion criteria. Specifically, the only ECG-related exclusion criterion in the
TAK-861-3001 protocol pertained to QTcF prolongation, and there were no exclusion criteria
specific to T wave inversion. The Applicant further noted that the participant had no relevant
cardiac medical history that would have precluded enrollment. Importantly, the finding
resolved by the end of the study, with the Week 12 ECG reported as normal, and the
participant successfully completed the trial. This clinical reviewer considers the Applicant's
response satisfactory.
(b) (6)

Of note, when each pivotal trial was assessed separately, no trends were observed. For
example, the slightly higher percentage of longer PR intervals and shorter QRS durations in
the oveporexton group was due to a few subjects in Study 3001. Study 3002 had percentages
comparable to placebo.

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 220860
Orzeyful (Oveporexton)
Other Integrated Summary of Safety Studies
TAK-861-2003
In TAK-861-2003, no clinically significant ECG abnormalities were reported.
90-Day Safety Update
Two clinically significant ECG abnormalities were reported since the original submission:
•

A 46-year-old female participant had ECGs on trial Days 14 and 28 showing first-degree
atrioventricular (AV) block, which were assessed as abnormal and clinically significant.
However, the participant had a documented medical history of first-degree AV heart block
(b) (6)
dating to
.

The ECG findings observed during the trial are consistent with her preexisting condition and
do not represent a new or treatment-emergent abnormality.
•

A 37-year-old male participant had a markedly abnormal PR interval (>200 msec) on trial
Day 182, with a recorded value of 203 msec, which was assessed as abnormal and clinically
significant. The participant's medical history included gastroesophageal reflux disease,
pharyngitis, obesity, Achilles tendinitis, obstructive sleep apnea syndrome, lumbar
spondylolisthesis, and seasonal allergies. The ECG was interpreted as first-degree AV block,
and a corresponding adverse event was reported and assessed as mild and related to trial
intervention.

We cannot exclude whether this TEAE was related to the drug. However, the finding
represents a nonserious, asymptomatic, isolated ECG abnormality with no evidence of
progression to higher-grade AV block, and does not raise further concerns.
Cardiac Safety-Interdisciplinary Review Team (IRT) Consult
Per the Cardiac Safety-Interdisciplinary Review Team (IRT) consult review dated December 18,
2024, of the cardiac safety report for Study TAK-861-1001 (QT evaluation report):
•

“TAK-861 did not prolong the QTcF interval in this clinical QTc assessment based on a Phase
1 study (TAK-861-1001) – see Table 1 for overall results. The data from the QTc assessment
can be used as a substitute for a thorough QT study (ICH E14 Q&A 5.1).”

•

“We agree that TAK-861 does not have a QTc prolonging effect. Therefore, a dedicated TQT
study is not necessary, and routine ECG monitoring as clinically indicated is acceptable for
clinical studies.”

In summary, the ECG safety profile of oveporexton in the pivotal trial pool appears
acceptable. Mean changes from baseline in ECG parameters at Week 12 were small and
comparable across treatment groups.
Although slightly higher percentages of oveporexton-treated subjects had markedly abnormal
PR intervals and shorter QRS durations compared to placebo, none were assessed as clinically

Orzeyful (Oveporexton)
significant. These findings were not dose-related and were driven by a few subjects in Study
3001; Study 3002 showed percentages comparable to placebo.
One participant with a preexisting clinically significant abnormal ECG at baseline was enrolled
and maintained abnormal findings through Week 8. An IR has been sent to clarify the
rationale for this enrollment. Given the preexisting condition, these findings are unlikely
related to study drug.
The Cardiac Safety-IRT concluded that oveporexton does not prolong the QTcF interval based
on the Phase 1 QTc assessment study, which can substitute for a thorough QT study per ICH
E14 Q&A 5.1.
In conclusion, overall, the ECG data do not raise significant safety concerns for oveporexton.

A few additional cases of TEAEs potentially related to drug hypersensitivity were reported in

hypersensitivity were observed at a higher rate in the oveporexton group compared to
placebo, and exclusively in the 2-mg BID arm, raising the question of whether these events
could represent true hypersensitivity responses to oveporexton.

Analysis of Submission-Specific Safety Issues
Adverse Events of Special Interest
In part due to issues with other drugs in development in the same class, the Applicant
identified adverse events of special interest (AESI) for oveporexton that included insomnia,
hepatic function abnormalities, urinary symptoms, visual disturbances, and psychiatric
disorders, including suicidal ideation and behavior. These AESIs were selected based on
mechanistic, pharmacological class, and clinical safety considerations. Insomnia was identified
as an AESI given the mechanism of action of oveporexton as an orexin receptor agonist, since
dysregulation of the orexin system plays a central role in sleep-wake regulation. Hepatic
function abnormalities were designated as an AESI because a prior investigational agent with
a similar mechanism of action was associated with reported cases of drug-induced liver injury
(DILI), leading to the interruption of its development. Urinary symptoms were included as an
AESI due to a recognized class effect, as urinary frequency has been observed with other
medications acting on the OX2 receptor, though the exact pathophysiologic mechanism of
voiding dysfunction with OX2R antagonism remains unclear. Visual disturbances were
included based on reports of such events with another agent in the same pharmacological
class. Psychiatric disorders, including suicidal ideation and behavior, were included given that
oveporexton is a new molecular entity acting on the central nervous system.
Hepatic function abnormalities and urinary symptoms are addressed separately by the
Division of Hepatology (DILI team) and the Division of Urology and Gynecology (DUOG),
respectively; the reader is referred to their corresponding consult reviews for a detailed
discussion of these events. Briefly, the DILI team review found no clinically significant
hepatotoxicity of concern in the phase 3 or long-term extension trials for oveporexton, no
cases of DILI were identified, there were no substantial imbalances in liver-related adverse
events or enzyme elevations versus placebo, and all elevated aminotransferase cases were
attributed to non-DILI causes (primarily myopathy, acetaminophen use, or mononucleosis).
The DUOG review found that urologically-relevant TEAEs were common across the clinical
program, with urinary frequency (pollakiuria) occurring in approximately 57% and urinary
urgency (micturition urgency) in 16% of oveporexton-treated patients in the pivotal trials
versus 5% and 1% in placebo, respectively; these events were generally mild-to-moderate,
onset was typically within 1 to 2 days of treatment initiation, and they are consistent with the
drug's on-target pharmacological mechanism.
Insomnia and psychiatric TEAEs and visual disturbances are discussed in the sections that
follow.
8.2.5.1.

Insomnia

Pivotal Trial Pool

Insomnia was among the most frequently reported AESIs in the oveporexton treatment
groups. As shown in Table 100, a notably high proportion of participants experienced at least
one insomnia event during the study: 55.0% in the 1-mg BID group and 60.3% in the 2-mg BID
273
Reference ID: 5847402

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 220860
Orzeyful (Oveporexton)
group, yielding an overall rate of 58.7% across all oveporexton-treated participants,
compared to only 1.3% in the placebo group. Although the rate appeared slightly higher in
the 2-mg BID group relative to the 1-mg BID group, the difference between dosages is small
and should be interpreted with caution, as it is unclear whether it represents a true doseresponse relationship or reflects random variability.

Regarding the onset and duration of insomnia events (Table 101; data obtained from
participants’ diaries), the majority of events began within the first 2 days of treatment
initiation, with approximately 90% of insomnia events in the oveporexton overall group
starting within Days 1 and 2, and an additional ~4% starting between Days 2 and 7. This early
onset pattern suggests that insomnia is likely a pharmacodynamic effect of the drug,
consistent with its mechanism of action as an orexin receptor agonist. In terms of duration,
most insomnia events were short-lived, with approximately 63% resolving within the first
week of onset. A smaller proportion lasted between 1 and 8 weeks. Notably, however,
approximately 17% of insomnia events were still ongoing at the time of study completion,
indicating that for a subset of participants, insomnia persisted throughout the duration of the
trial. Importantly, none of these insomnia events resulted in study discontinuation or were
classified as serious adverse events.

With respect to the nature of insomnia symptoms experienced (Table 102) (data obtained
from participants’ diaries), among participants who experienced at least one insomnia event,
the most commonly reported symptoms were trouble falling asleep (83.5% in the
oveporexton overall group) and trouble staying asleep (76.5%), with more than half of
affected participants (59.1%) experiencing both simultaneously. Approximately 55% of
participants also reported trouble falling back asleep after waking up. While 34.8% of
participants reported feeling bothered by their insomnia, only 14.8% reported that their
daytime activities were affected, suggesting that the functional impact of insomnia on daily
life was limited for the majority of those affected.

Other Integrated Summary of Safety Studies
TAK-861-2003
In the LTE study (TAK-861-2003), new insomnia events were reported by a relatively small
proportion of participants (68 [19.1%]). Of note, approximately half of these events occurred
275
Reference ID: 5847402

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 220860
Orzeyful (Oveporexton)
in participants who had been on placebo during the parent trials and were therefore being
exposed to oveporexton for the first time in the LTE. Additionally, some events may be
attributable to dosage adjustments during the extension period. Consistent with findings
from the pivotal studies, the onset of new insomnia events in the LTE was typically within the
first 1 to 2 days of initiating or modifying treatment, further supporting the early
pharmacodynamic nature of this effect.
With regard to symptom burden, the functional impact of insomnia in the LTE appeared
limited. The majority of participants who reported new insomnia events (71.1%) indicated
that their daytime activities were not affected, and approximately half (46.7%) did not find
the insomnia bothersome. Although a proportion of events resolved within the first 7 days,
some were longer in duration. The interpretation of event duration in the LTE is subject to
certain limitations, including the potential confounding effect of dosage changes, the overall
lower number of new events reported, and differences in the timing of adverse event
collection relative to the pivotal trials.
90-Day Safety Update
All new insomnia events (middle insomnia, initial insomnia) reported since the original
submission were mild to moderate in intensity and resolved. No participants have
discontinued trial intervention due to insomnia events since the original submission.
Overall, although insomnia was reported at a strikingly high frequency in oveporextontreated participants (~58% versus 1.3% in placebo), the available data suggest that these
events were predominantly short-lasting and of limited clinical impact. Most events resolved
within the first week, and none resulted in study discontinuation or were classified as serious.
Notably, despite more than one-third of affected participants reporting feeling bothered by
their insomnia, fewer than 15% indicated that their daytime activities were meaningfully
affected. Overall, both participants and principal investigators appear to have considered
these events manageable and not sufficiently burdensome to warrant stopping treatment.
Given the high frequency of insomnia relative to placebo, insomnia will be included in the
Warnings and Precautions section of labeling to ensure that prescribers are informed of this
risk and can counsel patients appropriately.
8.2.5.2.

Psychiatric Disorders

Pivotal Trial Pool

Beyond insomnia, this reviewer conducted a detailed assessment of PTs related to, or
potentially related to, psychiatric disorders. This evaluation included also PTs captured under
other SOC domains that may nonetheless have psychiatric relevance—for example,
psychomotor hyperactivity (coded under Musculoskeletal Disorders) and cataplexy (coded
under Nervous System Disorders). In the pivotal trial pool, single cases of depressive
symptoms, anhedonia, nervousness, restlessness, psychomotor hyperactivity, anxiety, and
hypnagogic hallucination were reported in the oveporexton groups, with no such events
observed in the placebo group. Cataplexy was reported in both treatment arms (n=4, 2% in
276
Reference ID: 5847402

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 220860
Orzeyful (Oveporexton)
the oveporexton group and n=1, 1.3% in the placebo group), which is not unexpected given
the underlying narcolepsy diagnosis in this population. As anticipated, somnolence/
hypersomnia was reported exclusively in the placebo group (n=2, 2.6%), consistent with the
expected pharmacological effect of oveporexton.
Other Integrated Summary of Safety Studies
TAK-861-2003
Beyond the self-injurious behavior already discussed in the C-SSRS and suicidal behavior
section, six participants (1.7%) reported anxiety, and one participant each reported major
depression (0.3%) and stress (0.3%).
90-Day Safety Update
Neither depression nor anxiety was mentioned in the 90-Day Safety Update.
In summary, the small sample sizes across treatment groups limit the ability to draw
meaningful conclusions from these individual events. When considering PTs that may
collectively reflect anxiety-related symptoms, using FDA query criteria, no difference in
anxiety was noted. Overall, the available data do not suggest that oveporexton has a
meaningful adverse effect on psychiatric domains beyond insomnia, which is discussed
separately. Continued monitoring of psychiatric TEAEs in the post-marketing setting would
nonetheless be prudent given that this is a new CNS-acting agent.
8.2.5.3.

Visual Disturbance

Pivotal Trial Pool

The percentage of visual disorders was similar between placebo and oveporexton groups
(2.6% in each group). However, examination of individual PTs reveals a potential signal for
vision blurred, which occurred in three patients (1.5%) in the oveporexton group compared to
0 cases in the placebo group (Table 103). Additionally, dry eye was reported in two patients
(1.0%) in the oveporexton group with no cases in placebo.
All these events were nonserious, mild to moderate, and did not result in trial intervention
discontinuation. No consistent patterns in latency or duration were observed. All events
resolved while participants were on trial treatment.

Other Integrated Summary of Safety Studies
TAK-861-2003
No safety concerns related to visual disturbance were identified with long-term oveporexton
exposure.
90-Day Safety Update
No safety concerns related to visual disturbance were identified with long-term oveporexton
exposure.
In summary, although vision blurred and dry eye occurred exclusively in the oveporexton
group, the overall safety profile of these events is reassuring. All cases were mild-tomoderate in severity, nonserious, self-limited, and did not lead to treatment discontinuation.
The absence of consistent temporal patterns and the resolution of all events during continued
treatment further support a favorable benefit-risk assessment.
This focused assessment of visual disturbances was conducted post hoc, after study
completion, following reports of visual safety signals with another molecule in the same class
from a different commercial development program. Consequently, the pivotal trials for
oveporexton did not include protocol-specified ophthalmologic assessments or systematic
visual function evaluations. The reported incidence of visual disturbances may therefore be
underestimated due to the reliance on spontaneous adverse event reporting without
dedicated ophthalmologic monitoring.
Nonetheless, at this time, these findings do not raise significant safety concerns. The
incidence and characteristics of visual disturbances can be monitored with routine postmarketing surveillance to ensure no emerging safety signals with broader exposure in realworld use, particularly given the limitations of the available clinical trial data in this domain.

Safety/Tolerability
See Section 8.2.4. Safety Results–C-SSRS for discussion of results related to C-SSRS.
Safety Analyses by Demographic Subgroups
As shown in Table 104, the overall incidence TEAEs was generally consistent across
demographic subgroups, with no evidence of meaningful heterogeneity in the safety profile
of oveporexton across the populations examined. (As a word of general caution, these are
278
Reference ID: 5847402

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 220860
Orzeyful (Oveporexton)
post hoc safety analyses relying on limited sample size populations and are therefore of
limited interpretability.)
With respect to sex, the incidence of any TEAE was broadly comparable between female (86%
for oveporexton 1 to 2 mg BID combined versus 54% for placebo) and male participants (88%
versus 44%), with risk differences of similar magnitude in both subgroups, suggesting no
meaningful differential in the overall adverse event burden by sex.
With respect to race, a meaningful comparison is limited to the White and Asian subgroups,
as all other racial categories (American Indian or Alaska Native, Black or African American,
Multiple, and Unknown) had sample sizes too small to draw reliable conclusions. Among
White participants, the incidence of any TEAE was 90% for oveporexton (1 to 2 mg BID
combined) versus 59% for placebo, and among Asian participants, 85% versus 46%, indicating
a broadly consistent pattern of adverse event incidence across these two racial subgroups.
Finally, the incidence of TEAEs was comparable between participants enrolled in the United
States (88% for oveporexton versus 50% for placebo) and those enrolled outside the United
States (87% versus 49%), suggesting that the safety profile of oveporexton is consistent across
geographic regions and is not driven by any country-specific effect.

Additional Safety Explorations

Human Carcinogenicity or Tumor Development
The Applicant did not provide human carcinogenicity data with this application.
During the review of the oveporexton clinical development program, two cases of breast
cancer and one case of acute myeloid leukemia were identified in the LTE study TAK-8612003, prompting an IR to the Applicant, asking them to report all cases of malignancy that
occurred across the development program. In response, the Applicant clarified that a total of
four malignancy cases had been identified (Table 105), all in the LTE study.
Table 105. Malignancy Cases in the Oveporexton Development Program

Cases were discussed with our experts in the Division of Oncology 1 (DO1) and the Division of
Epidemiology 1 (DEPI1). Across all disciplines, including the nonclinical team, the consensus is
that these cases are likely due to chance, for the following reasons:
•

Negative nonclinical carcinogenicity studies: Oveporexton was not carcinogenic in the
nonclinical studies.

•

Biological plausibility is not established: The four cases span three histologically and
etiologically distinct cancer types, all occurring within less than 2 years of drug exposure.
There is currently no known mechanism by which selective orexin 2 receptor agonism
would be expected to initiate or promote malignancy.

•

Background malignancy risk in the narcolepsy population: A nationwide population-based
cohort study by Tseng et al. (2015), reports a significantly elevated cancer risk in narcolepsy
patients compared to the general population, independent of pharmacological treatment.
This elevated background risk may account for the observed cases.
281

Reference ID: 5847402

NDA/BLA Multi-disciplinary Review and Evaluation NDA 220860
Orzeyful (Oveporexton)
An internal review of other premarketing studies evaluating drugs approved for patients with
narcolepsy (i.e., sodium oxybate, mixed oxybate salts, and pitolisant) also noted similar
frequencies of malignancy cases relative to this program, and no unusual post-marketing
trends.
Based on the evidence to date, no additional regulatory action is warranted at this time. The
Agency's conclusion is to continue routine postmarketing monitoring for malignancy as part
of standard pharmacovigilance
Human Reproduction and Pregnancy
The studies conducted in this clinical development program excluded patients who were
pregnant or who were unwilling to agree to use of contraception during study participation.
Postmarketing studies related to the use of oveporexton during pregnancy and lactation will
be required and are discussed in Section 13, Postmarketing Requirements and Commitment.
Pediatrics and Assessment of Effects on Growth
No clinical studies of oveporexton were conducted in the pediatric population. Three
participants aged 16 to 17 were enrolled in the adult pivotal studies; however, their exposure
to oveporexton and this sample was too limited to support any meaningful conclusions.
Overdose, Drug Abuse Potential, Withdrawal, and Rebound
The Controlled Substance Staff (CSS) team reviewed this application and did not note any
major abuse potential signals in the studies. They have recommended Schedule IV for
oveporexton based on the mechanism of action. See the CSS consult review for more details.
Safety in the Postmarket Setting
Safety Concerns Identified Through Postmarket Experience
Not applicable as the drug is an NME and first-in-class (recently approved in China and Japan,
not approved anywhere else globally) and not yet available for any indications.
Expectations on Safety in the Postmarket Setting
See Section 13, Postmarketing Requirements and Commitment. It is expected that use in the
postmarket setting will include treatment of a broader range of patients than those enrolled
in clinical trials (i.e., older patients and those with more medical comorbidities or
concomitant medication use). In addition to assessing for safety findings in the broader
general patient population, routine postmarketing pharmacovigilance will be important for
assessing for adverse reactions too rare to be detected in the clinical development program.
